iPhone 14 Pro / 13 Pro / 12 Pro A16 / A15 / A14 ❌ ✅ ✅ ✅ Fully supported
iPhone 14 / 13 / 12 A15 / A15 / A14 ❌ ✅ ✅ ❌ Fully supported. iPhone 12 = perf baseline
iPhone SE (3rd gen) A15 ❌ ✅ ✅ ❌ Supported; compact layout pass for map + mesh
iPhone 11 / 11 Pro A13 ❌ ✅ ✅ ❌ Best-effort; cap trajectories at 12
iPhone XR / XS A12 ❌ ✅ (hw) ✅ (hw) ❌ Unsupported — not validated
iPad / Android — — — — — Out of scope for v1 capture

On LiDAR: BRIEF §6 is explicit — an aid, never a requirement — and the physics forces it anyway. Apple's published range is ~5 m and a goal is 10–35 m away, so LiDAR contributes nothing to measuring the goal. It does exactly three near-field things inside the ritual. No screen, no state, and no cohort key branches on whether the device has LiDAR.

4.2 Backend

Modules by bounded context. Four are new (fields, insights, rankings, jk); adding them means editing apps/be-api/.importlinter in the same commit, because modules-are-independent lists modules explicitly and an unlisted module is unchecked, not forbidden.

Module Owns (tables) Responsibility
accounts user_profile, profile_change_log, deletion_request Who the user is, and whether they are verified
fields field_profile, field_calibration_check The registered venue + camera geometry. Produces the calibration_hash that defines "identical conditions"
training training_session, shot Sessions and shots. The shot row is the spine everything hangs off
analysis analysis_job, trajectory, pose, artifact, upload_session, upload_part, shot_dispute, validation_sample Talking to be-vision, ingesting bytes, the two result tables
insights insight, insight_shot Multi-shot coaching
progress benchmark Personal best · JK · STRYKE-verified, one shape, three scopes
rankings leaderboard_eligibility, cohort_percentile, cohort_dirty Verified-only percentiles and the anti-gaming gates
jk jk_lesson, jk_record, jk_challenge Lessons, records, challenge-under-matching-conditions
challenges keeper_model, keeper_challenge Goalkeeper simulation (P1)
core/ (no tables — pure functions) cohort.py (one distance_bin shared by cohort_key and cell_key), percentile.py, confidence.py, grade.py, insights.py, bands.py, session.py, geometry.py
schemas/ (pydantic only) Response shapes used by more than one module

Cross-module foreign keys are allowed at the database level and forbidden at the Python level. shot.field_profile_id is a real FK, declared by string so SQLAlchemy resolves it through Base.metadata without an import. When training needs field data it calls fields.service.get_profile(...). The same rule applies to schemas: ComparisonOut, EligibilityOut, SpeedOut, ArtifactUrlOut and NofM live in stryke_api/schemas/shared.py under a shared-schemas-are-leaf import-linter contract.

The honesty invariant is enforced in the schema, not in the UI:

  • shot.ball_speed_kmh is nullable and guarded by CHECK (ball_speed_kmh IS NULL OR claim_point_speed IS TRUE).
  • trajectory.apex_m and launch_elevation_deg are guarded by claim_apex / claim_elevation.
  • There is no spin_rpm or magnus_g column anywhere — spin is a type enum plus a confidence enum.
  • There is no overall_posture_score column. The absence of the column is how it stays forbidden.
  • No numeric field in a contract or a response model carries a non-null default. Not 0.0, not -1, not a plausible-looking constant such as a size-5 ball diameter. Enforced in CI by rg 'float = [0-9]' contracts/src returning nothing.

Auth. Supabase Auth, Apple Sign-In + email OTP. be-api validates the JWT on every request (JWKS cached 10 min) in exactly one dependency, accounts.deps.current_user. The app never talks to Postgres — PostgREST is disabled for public and be-api holds the only DB credentials. The scaffold's POST /training/sessions?user_id=… is deleted: user_id comes from the token.

RLS, done so that it actually fires. be-api connects with pooled service credentials and parses the JWT itself, so by default auth.uid() is NULL and a policy of user_id = auth.uid() filters out every row — the quietest possible failure. One mandatory request-scoped dependency makes the claim true:

@asynccontextmanager
async def request_session(user_id: UUID | None) -> AsyncIterator[AsyncSession]:
    """SET LOCAL is transaction-scoped, so a pooled connection cannot leak one request's
    identity into the next. This is why every handler runs inside a transaction, even reads."""
    async with SessionLocal() as session, session.begin():
        await session.execute(text("SET LOCAL ROLE stryke_app"))       # not service_role
        if user_id is not None:
            await session.execute(text("SET LOCAL request.jwt.claims = :claims"),
                                  {"claims": json.dumps({"sub": str(user_id)})})
        yield session

plus two guards: a startup assertion that current_user is neither service_role nor postgres outside tests, and a per-table integration test that inserts two users' rows, lists as user A and asserts a non-zero count — which converts "RLS returns nothing" from a production mystery into a CI failure. Background jobs run as service_role deliberately and never call request_session.

"Verified user" vs "verified shot" are two different things and a shot ranks only when both hold. A user is verified when: signed in with Apple or email confirmed; display_name, handle, dominant_foot and birth_year are set; at least one completed Ranked session on an active field profile; and not suspended with no open abuse review. Suspension is not absorbing — POST /v1/admin/accounts/{id}/reinstate sets registered, clears suspended_at and enqueues a full re-evaluation. A suspension with no documented exit is an account we can never un-break.

Worker deployment — three lanes, two resource classes, still two apps:

Lane Where Hardware Scaling
ball apps/be-vision, Modal function ball_lane CPU, 4 vCPU, 8 GB min_containers=1 (cents/day)
pose apps/be-vision, Modal function pose_lane 1× NVIDIA A10G min_containers=0, keep_warm(900) opened by POST /sessions
render apps/be-vision, Modal function render_lane 1× NVIDIA A10G min_containers=0, on tap only

Shot 1 pays the cold start; shots 2–N are warm; a session that ends releases the GPU. Cost is TARGET ≈ $0.01 of GPU per clip at target latency against a permanently warm box at ≈ $24/day, so scale-to-zero wins until roughly 1,900–2,400 shots/day (price says 2,400; a warm box saturates near 1,900 at 45 s each, so the real crossover is capacity). Re-check at 1,500 shots/day.

analysis_job is a be-api-owned dispatch ledger, not a queue the worker polls. The worker never writes a job state and never opens a database connection. The worker's loop is:

  1. Read the version before validating. AnalysisJob.model_validate(payload) on a v1 payload raises before any version check can run, and the exception handler would turn that into failure_class=INFRASTRUCTURE, which is retried three times, failing identically each time. So: read contract_version from the raw body, dispatch to AnalysisJob or AnalysisJobV1.upgrade(...), and return failure_class=CONTRACT for anything else.
  2. POST /internal/analysis/jobs/{id}/events at every stage boundary and every 10 s.
  3. Write artifacts to output_prefix with the service-role key.
  4. POST /internal/analysis/jobs/{id}/result with an AnalysisResult.
  5. Never raise out of the loop — an exception becomes AnalysisResult(status=FAILED, failure_class=INFRASTRUCTURE). The API decides whether to retry.

be-api's result handler mirrors step 1 and upgrades AnalysisResultV1 — deliberately, to a REFUSED result with an explicit reason, because fabricating a BallFlight from ShotObservation.speed_mps is exactly the bug this whole design exists to prevent.

What the import-linter actually guarantees: apps/be-vision/.importlinter has one contract, worker-never-imports-api. It does not enforce "the worker imports nothing but contracts" — the worker imports torch, cv2 and numpy and always will. Say the true thing.

4.3 CV pipeline

What exists today: two separately-shipped modules in one repo that do not yet talk to each other — worker/cli.py (GVHMR → SMPL-X world sequence → kick metrics → hero renders → results.json v2, E2E-validated 2026-08-13) and pipeline/ball.py (2,142 lines, branch feat/ball-trajectory; monocular 2D track → metric 3D fit → ball_flight.json with an honest band, not wired into the worker).

Seven stages. The mandatory per-shot path is S1–S6; S7 is lazy.

session video (continuous, N shots)  ── device emits one clip per shot ──►
  │
  ├─ S1 window validation ──────────► accepted window [t_start, t_end] + clip_window_s trim
  │      │        (server-side PRODUCER only on the P1 photo_library import path)
  │      └── per shot, two lanes in parallel:
  │            CPU:  S2 ball 2D track ──► track.json (uv, radius, conf, src)
  │                  S3 contact frame ──► contact_frame (ball departure, refined by foot)
  │                  S4 metric 3D fit ──► points_3d in GOAL frame + band + claims
  │            GPU:  S5 pose (GVHMR) ───► SMPL-X world sequence
  │                  S6 impact posture ─► angles{} at contact + pose_confidence
  │
  └─ on tap ─► S7 renders ───────────► hero video, mesh.glb, trajectory overlay
Stage State Refuses to output
S1 window validation device-side producer exists; server validator is new a window whose ball never departs; a launch outside the shooting zone; overlapping windows (multiple_balls_in_flight → refuse both)
S2 ball 2D track exists (ball.py substages 1–2) < 8 real detections → hard RuntimeError ("only N real detections — refusing to fit"). 8–19 → only_N_detections_speed_is_band_only and the point value is refused downstream. A "real" detection is conf ≥ 0.3; interpolated points carry 0.15 and are never counted. Track truncates after 6 consecutive misses rather than reporting noise
S3 contact frame ball-side exists; foot cross-check is new disagreement > 33 ms → contact_source: "disagreed", point speed refused, c_pose = 0
S4 metric 3D fit exists (ball.py substage 4) speed_kmh = null unless all of: a metric reference was used, the time base is trusted, and ≥ 20 real detections. apex_m is claimable only when a single basin survives and a metric reference exists. claims.spin_magnitude is hard-wired false
S5 pose (GVHMR) exists (pipeline/motion.py) more than one primary subject; a window where the kicker's feet leave the frame at contact
S6 impact posture exists (pipeline/metrics.py) any angle whose defining joints were not tracked at contact; shoulderHipRotation when the shoulder or hip line is within 15° of the camera axis (UNMEASURED threshold — set it in the repeatability study)
S7 renders exists (render.py, export.py); the GLB bake does not a trajectory overlay with a speed burn-in when claims.point_speed is false. The arc itself is always renderable — shape is one tier more robust than scale

S3's tolerance is in milliseconds, not frames, and that matters. Three frames at 240 fps is 12.5 ms — a better contact estimate than one frame at 60 fps (16.7 ms) — yet a frame count would score it worse:

Disagreement Verdict @ 60 fps @ 240 fps
≤ 17 ms agreed, c_pose = 1.0 ≤ 1 frame ≤ 4 frames
17–33 ms tolerated, c_pose = 0.5 2 frames 5–8 frames
> 33 ms disagreed, point speed refused, c_pose = 0 ≥ 3 frames ≥ 9 frames

Contact comes from the ball, refined by the foot; not the reverse. The ball's last at-rest frame is a step change in a quantity we measure directly; peak foot speed is an argmax over a smoothed 3D joint trajectory and moves with pose noise. refine_contact already does this with the fused-track acceleration spike. (04 §9.5's "contact agreed within 2 frames" restates this in ms.)

The fit's eight parameters, in full, because two were missing from earlier drafts and one is load-bearing:

Parameter Bound Note
h camera height [0.8, 4.0] m fixed in Verified mode
tilt camera tilt down [0°, 45°] fixed in Verified mode
v0 launch speed [2, 60] m/s rails on the goal-anchored example
el launch elevation [−10°, 80°] rails on the un-anchored example
az launch azimuth [−60°, 60°] → must widen to [−100°, 100°] structurally blocks side-on capture
g_eff [3, 20] m/s² rails on the goal-anchored example; fixed at 9.81 in Verified
aL lateral Magnus [−14, 14] m/s² rails on both shipped examples
aV vertical Magnus [−14, 14] m/s² only under --magnus full

Task S4-1 — widen _P_BOUNDS["az"] to ±100°, before Run 1 is filmed. _launch_state builds the launch velocity as v0 · [cos(el)·sin(az), sin(el), cos(el)·cos(az)] in the camera frame with +z camera-forward, so az = 0 is a ball flying directly away from the camera and az = ±90° is side-on. A ±60° box makes a true side-on shot unrepresentable: the solver rails az, c_rail = 0, and the shot is auto-refused for a reason that has nothing to do with the clip. Until it lands, the framing gate's best condition reads as a catastrophic failure.

A railed fit is a rejected fit in v1. When the solver settles on a bound, railed_parameters() names it; today that is only a warning. Both shipped example outputs rail — the un-anchored reference clip rails tilt at 45° and el at −10°; the goal-anchored Stanford clip rails v0 at 60 m/s, g_eff at 20 and aL at −14.

Anchors: goal PnP (the only accepted metric reference) → ball apparent diameter. There is no gravity anchor. It was removed on 2026-08-21 (commit 36d6aa0) because the solved effective g came back at 14.69 m/s², 1.5× real gravity — "a scale error wearing gravity's clothes". The honest consequence: the reference clip's band widened from [22.9, 48.5] to [28.8, 76.6] (source: ~/Desktop/programming/stryke-pipeline/docs/BALL.md, end-of-file removal note). Gravity is still in the dynamics; it is no longer a ruler.

Latency budget, rebuilt from labelled parts (per shot, warm):

Stage Today v1 target Basis
S1 validation — ≤ 1 s / min of session TARGET, stage is new
S2 + S4 ball (CPU) ~17 s ~17 s measured, whole module, 5 s / 264-frame clip (source: …/23-stryke-heroshoot/README.md log 2026-08-19). The ≤ 4 s / ≤ 6 s per-substage figures are TARGETs needing a ~2× speedup nothing here justifies — do not budget on them
S3 contact — ≤ 0.3 s TARGET
S5 preprocess (GPU) 39.3 s 8–12 s measured cold / TARGET warm (source: …/23-stryke-heroshoot/README.md §3)
S5 GVHMR motion (GPU) 0.63 s 0.63 s measured — 21× real-time; WHAM's 86.8 s was the baseline it replaced (source: ~/Desktop/programming/opencap-monocular report)
S6 metrics ~0 s ~0 s measured
Model load 17.9–25 s 0 warm measured
Container start (models baked in) ~120 s same measured
S7 render, on tap ~30 s for 2 styles; 117 s cold 8–15 s for 1 style MEASURED / TARGET
Path Today After the preprocess pass
Per shot S1–S6, serial ~57 s ~29 s
Per shot S1–S6, ball on CPU ∥ pose on GPU ≤ 40 s ≤ 25 s ← this is the design
Marginal shot in a 10-shot session ≤ 20 s ≤ 15 s
On tap (S7) ≤ 30 s ≤ 15 s
Resolved

Resolved: the "~25 s" figure. It is the v1 target column of the heroshoot README's own "measured baseline → targets" table, not a measurement, and it spans upload→render — including the hero render this pipeline moves off the mandatory path entirely. The measured complete cold run is motion 82 s + metrics 0 s + renders 117 s + export 0.1 s ≈ 199 s; the README's "~110 s cold" is a planning projection that does not equal the sum of its own log's stages. The number to build against today is ≤ 40 s worker-side, and 05 Stage 2's "40 s, not 25 s" is the acceptance number.

Frame-count assumption, stated once because everything scales with it. All the timings above are for a 264-frame clip. A STRYKE shot clip is 3.5 s — at 240 fps that is 840 frames, 3.2× the reference — so the device writes a decimation manifest into the sidecar and the two lanes consume different streams:

Lane Stream Frames for a 3.5 s shot Why
Ball every frame within ±0.4 s of contact, every 4th frame outside ~330 at 240 fps, ~230 at 4K60 Keeps the reference clip's frame count so the MEASURED ~17 s holds. Contact frames are the weakest detections and anchor launch speed — they must not be decimated
Pose a uniform 30 fps decimation 105 GVHMR runs at 30 fps upstream; preprocess is the per-frame cost

The manifest is a list of source frame indices plus their original PTS, so decimation never touches the time base — timebase.trusted survives it.

Session-level consequence (DERIVED): twenty shots, ball concurrency 4 → ~85 s to drain, so arcs fill the map inside ~2 minutes of the last kick; pose on one warm GPU worker → ~500 s ≈ 8.3 min, so mesh tiles fill in over ~8 minutes. ⇒ The live capture screen shows shot markers, never trajectories. The accumulated map is the post-session review screen, and partial is what makes it usable.

Coordinate frames — four, and only one is the product frame.

Goal frame G  (canonical, product-facing)
  origin  O_G = midpoint of the two post bases, on the ground
  +X_G    along the goal line, to the shooter's right when facing the goal
  +Y_G    up (anti-gravity)
  +Z_G    out of the goal mouth, toward the field / the shooter
  units   metres, right-handed
Product quantity Definition in G
Placement (X, Y) at the first Z_G = 0 crossing. Mouth is X ∈ [−W/2, +W/2], Y ∈ [0, H]; full size W = 7.32, H = 2.44
On target crossing inside the mouth rectangle
Shot distance hypot(X_launch, Z_launch) — ground distance from launch to goal centre. Binned at 2 m for the cohort key
Placement zones 3×3 grid of the mouth; cell edges at X = ±W/6, Y = H/3, 2H/3
Apex max(Y) along points_3d — claimable only per S4

B is the ball-fit frame ball.py emits when there is no goal anchor: origin at the launch point, +x camera-right, +y up, +z camera-forward. B is not gravity-aligned by construction — "up" is up only because the solver's tilt came out right, and on the reference clip that parameter railed at its 45° bound. W is ARKit's gravity-aligned session world; C is the ARKit camera frame.

Twenty shots in B are twenty arcs each starting at its own origin in its own tilted frame. Stacking them draws a flower, not a shot map. So:

Session Frame of points_3d What the scene draws
Ranked (field profile, ARKit ritual) G The full accumulated map: all arcs, goal, arrival markers, shooting zone
Quick with declared goal dims + corner taps G, recovered by goal PnP The accumulated map, Estimated on every number
Quick without a goal B One shot at a time, in isolation, over its own video; no goal, no shared ground

<ShotScene> refuses to place a trajectory that is not in G rather than drawing it at the origin, and TrajectoryOut / ShotMapItem carry a frame: "G" | "B" discriminator so the app never infers it from the mode. ShotMapOut returns only frame-G shots and lists the rest in unrenderable with reason='trajectory_frame_b'.

Why the focal matters more than anything else. scene_measured.json contains a focal_family table for one real clip: the same measured pixels give a goal depth of 14.45 m and a launch speed of 44.1 km/h at f = 500 px, and 37.58 m / 104.6 km/h at f = 1300 px — a 2.4× speed span from focal alone (source: …/stryke-pipeline/out/goal_anchored/scene_measured.json). That family exists precisely because the goal in that clip is fronto-parallel; the file says so itself: "fronto-parallel: post asymmetry 1.93 %, crossbar 1.44 deg → cannot solve focal, only depth at a given focal". Four coplanar corners viewed square-on cannot separate focal from depth. There are two independent fixes and we want both: pin f from the device's per-frame intrinsics (the Verified-mode fix), and make the goal non-coplanar so PnPf solves focal on its own (which is why the ritual coaches an elevated, oblique camera).

The basin degeneracy, with the model setting each number came from — because quoting only the worse one overstates the defect:

Model setting Basin outcome on the synthetic clip (truth: 30.0°, 1.6 m)
--magnus off four basins spanning 1.4–3.2 m and 16.8–29.5° within 0.1 px RMS; truth was rank 4 of 4 (source: ~/Desktop/programming/stryke-pipeline/docs/BALL.md §Validation)
--magnus lateral (today's default) truth at rank 1 (1.66 m / 12.4°, 29.4°)

On the reference clip under the shipped default the post-Tier-1 fit still produces 4 basins spanning 7.7–26.2° elevation and 0.14–0.71 m apex within 0.5 px. The cause is named: removing the lateral DOF from the geometry solve, on a clip with a real curl, lets camera height and tilt bend to absorb it. So the degeneracy is real and is why claims.apex is false — and the gate (basin_ambiguous == false on ≥ 90 % of Verified clips) is baselined under --magnus lateral, with the flag recorded in the run README.

Licensing — two encumbrances, two separate answers.

Row Component Licence The only paths
A SMPL / SMPL-X body model MPI research-only Meshcapade licence — money and a contract; clears the model for commercial use, zero code change for this half
B GVHMR code custom non-commercial, copyleft-on-modification A written commercial exception from the authors · replace the backend with SAM 3D Body (commercial-permitted SAM licence, MHR rig, no OpenSim bridge yet) · replace with GEM-X (Apache-2.0 code + NVIDIA Open Model weights — the weights licence is the half that decides commercial use) · ship the ball half only

A Meshcapade licence alone does not make the shipped pipeline commercially clean. It clears row A and leaves row B untouched. The ball stack is the opposite and stays clean: the 2D detector layer is MIT (WASB-SBDT, TrackNetV3, BlurBall), but every metric-3D asset surveyed is GPL / NC / unlicensed — read the papers, reimplement the ideas, never vendor the code. pipeline/ball.py has no GVHMR dependency at all ("Pure CPU: cv2 + numpy + scipy"), so ball speed, trajectory and placement survive any licensing outcome. Separately, USPTO 10796437 sits close to our claim set and an FTO review belongs before any marketed speed number.

The swap is contained only if the joint-name-keyed boundary lands first. Today pipeline/metrics.py indexes joints by SMPL-X integer index and pipeline/render.py imports SMPLX_PARENTS. The port goes in apps/be-vision; stryke-pipeline keeps the GVHMR/SMPL-X implementation and is wrapped by an adapter:

# apps/be-vision/src/.../ports/pose.py  — one port, two real implementations
class PoseBackend(Protocol):
    def run(self, video: Path, workdir: Path) -> PoseResult: ...

@dataclass
class PoseResult:
    joints: dict[str, np.ndarray]   # NAME -> (n,3) world metres, ground at y=0. Names are OURS.
    mesh: MeshSequence | None       # verts/faces/parents — renderer-only, never metrics
    fps: float
    rig: str                        # "smplx" | "mhr" | "gemx"
    per_frame_conf: np.ndarray

With that in place metrics.py reads joints["r_ankle"] and never learns an index, and the swap is one new backend plus a rig-specific parent list in the renderer. PoseResult on the wire carries body_model and model_license_class, and ArtifactRef.shareable is false when model_license_class == 'noncommercial' — that is what makes the licence gate enforceable at the API rather than in a wiki page somebody forgets to read.

Confidence — the inputs. Every term is stored in confidence_inputs as a value or an explicit null, never a neutral default.

Ball / scale terms (feed speed_confidence):

Term Source Value
c_ref metric reference quality.metric_reference 1.0 Ranked w/ goal PnP · 0.75 Quick w/ goal dims · 0.35 diameter only
c_time time base timebase.trusted, fps_uncertainty 1 − min(fps_uncertainty, 0.5)/0.5 × 0.6
c_det detections n_detections 0 if < 8 (refused) · 0.3 + 0.7·(n−8)/12 for 8–20 · 1.0 above 20
c_rms fit residual fit.rms_px / ball pixel diameter at contact clip(1 − rms_px/(2·d_ball_px), 0, 1)
c_basin geometry uniqueness basin_ambiguous, len(basin_solutions) 1.0 single basin · 1/len(basins) otherwise
c_anchor anchor agreement per_anchor None unless ≥ 2 independent non-null anchors exist
c_rail rails railed_parameters 0.0 if non-empty
c_cal calibration closure_grade 1.0 good · 0.6 degraded · 0 rejected · 0.5 Quick (no profile)

Body / capture terms (feed pose_confidence; c_frame feeds both):

Term Source Value
c_frame framing the framing gate 1.0 all PASS · 0.8 per WARN, multiplied · 0 on any BLOCK
c_pose contact agreement S3, in ms 1.0 ≤ 17 ms · 0.5 17–33 ms · 0 > 33 ms
c_kp keypoint quality GVHMR per-frame confidence, mean over contact ± 3 frames the mean, clipped
c_subj subject S5's refusals 1.0 single primary subject with feet in frame at contact · 0.0 otherwise

c_anchor is None far more often than it looks. With gravity gone there are two anchors — and on a no-goal clip, one. A naive clip(1 − spread/0.20, 0, 1) on a single value gives spread 0 → c_anchor = 1.0: a perfect agreement score awarded exactly when there is nothing to agree with. Both shipped artifacts prove it — {"diameter_kmh": 57.4, "goal_kmh": null} with a 4.5×-wide band, and {"diameter_kmh": 216.0, "goal_kmh": 216.0} with 10 detections and three railed parameters (source: …/out/unanchored/ball_flight.json, …/out/goal_anchored/ball_flight.json). Three rules replace it: c_anchor = None below two non-null anchors; identical per-anchor values count as one anchor (if |a − b| / mean < 0.5 %, treat as one — equality is evidence of shared conditioning, since anchored() re-solves with the camera pinned to a fit that already consumed the radius observations); and the soft weights renormalise over the terms that are present.

# ---- speed / scale ----
HARD_SPEED = (c_rail, c_frame, c_cal)     # any zero → the number is not analysable as claimed
SOFT_SPEED = {"ref": 0.30, "time": 0.15, "det": 0.15, "rms": 0.10, "basin": 0.15, "anchor": 0.15}
present = {k: w for k, w in SOFT_SPEED.items() if c[k] is not None}
scale   = sum(present.values())
speed_confidence = min(HARD_SPEED) * sum(w / scale * c[k] for k, w in present.items())

# ---- body / posture. NO calibration term: posture does not depend on ball scale. ----
HARD_POSE = (c_pose, c_subj)
pose_confidence = min(HARD_POSE) * (0.5 * c_kp + 0.5 * c_frame)

Worked, so the tier bands can be checked rather than trusted (c_time = c_det = c_rms = c_basin = 1.0, framing all PASS, no rails):

Shot c_ref c_anchor soft min(HARD) speed_confidence tier
Ranked, goal PnP 1.00 0.9 0.985 1.0 0.985 A
Quick, goal dims declared 0.75 0.9 0.910 0.5 (c_cal) 0.455 B
Quick, no goal 0.35 None 0.771 0.5 0.385 C
Any shot with a railed parameter — — — 0.0 0.0 D
Resolved

Resolved: one confidence score or two. A single analysis_confidence with c_cal as a HARD term capped every Quick shot at 0.5, and the coaching window needed ≥ 0.55 — so no Quick shot could ever be coached, contradicting BRIEF §5 (Quick is excluded from percentiles and rankings and nothing else). It was also a category error: coaching metrics are posture angles and do not depend on ball scale, so gating them on a calibration closure residual gates a body measurement on a ball measurement. Two scores. 04's shot.analysis_confidence column becomes speed_confidence + pose_confidence, and core/insights.py reads the second.

The weights are initial engineering values; task V-1 refits them against the Run 2 radar set by regressing |error vs radar| on the terms. Until then they are labelled as estimates in the report and never shown to users. verified_eligible is a stored boolean, not recomputed at query time — the cohort a shot belongs to must not silently change when a threshold does; re-deriving it is a migration with a version stamp.

4.4 3D rendering

The visual language is the shipped renderer's constants, not a mood board. The two hero renders (opencap-pages/assets/stryke_mesh.mp4, stryke_hologram.mp4) were produced by stryke-pipeline/pipeline/render.py, so the in-app 3D view is built from that file's numbers — or a server-rendered share video and the interactive view on the next screen will look like two different products.

Parameter Value
Background bg_color = [8,9,12,255] → #08090C (a sampler reads #07090B off the mp4; that is the h264 yuv420p round-trip, not a different intent)
Ambient [0.16, 0.17, 0.20]
Floor 14 × 0.02 × 14 m box, top at y = 0; baseColor #1C1F26, metallic 0.30, roughness 0.60, flat-shaded
Output 720 × 900 (4:5), supersample = 2 then Lanczos down; libx264, yuv420p, crf 20
Key light (warm) [1.00,0.97,0.92], intensity 3.6, at +[2.5, 3.5, 2.0] from the smoothed root
Rim light (cool) [0.55,0.70,1.00], intensity 3.2, at +[-2.5, 2.2, -2.8]
Fill [0.90,0.90,1.00], intensity 1.1, at +[0, 1.0, 3.5]
Body mesh #6B8CD9, metallic 0.35, roughness 0.35, opaque, smooth-shaded
Body hologram #5A8CF2 @ 0.22, metallic 0.10, roughness 0.60, alphaMode="BLEND"
Bone #FF8C26, emissive #FF801F, cylinder r = 0.016 m, 12 sections
Joint #66BFFF, emissive #59B3FF, icosphere subdivisions=2, r = 0.028 m
Skeleton 22 joints, SMPLX_PARENTS for the 21 bones (source: …/stryke-pipeline/pipeline/render.py, pipeline/motion.py:20)
Camera az = −58° → −12° over the clip (max(n-1, 1) guard), eye = target + [3.7·sin az, 1.05, 3.7·cos az], look = target + [0, 0.12, 0], fovY 41°, target y pinned to 0.95, root smoothing tgt[i] = 0.85·tgt[i-1] + 0.15·joints[i,0]

The warm key against the cool rim is what makes the pearl body read as metal rather than plastic; sampled off frame 40 the body spans #5F6D8A (shadow) → #97A9D0 (lit) → #DFEDFB (specular). Do not flatten it to one colour. The emissive term is what makes the skeleton survive on the near-black floor.

Resolved

Resolved: the 3D palette. 02 §5.1 gave a different value for every 3D colour and instructed an additive bloom pass; 06 §2.1 transcribes render.py. 06 wins and 02 §5.1's table is deleted — its values were eyeballed from the compressed videos, and it invented a clearcoat and a sheen term pyrender.MetallicRoughnessMaterial does not have, so the server could never have matched them. There is no post-process bloom: the glow is the emissive term, and adding a bloom pass on device is the fastest way to make the app look different from the share video the user just posted. clearcoat 0.6 is recorded as a deliberate non-adoption rather than an oversight — pyrender has no clearcoat term, so adding it on device would break the match.

One artefact both sides read. Promote the constants: add contracts/src/stryke_contracts/render.py with RENDER_SPEC_VERSION and a frozen RenderSpec; pipeline/render.py imports it instead of declaring STYLES / BONE_MAT / JOINT_MAT / FLOOR_MAT inline; make contracts emits apps/fe-mobile/src/render/renderSpec.generated.ts. The app must never hand-type these numbers. AnalysisResult gains render_spec_version, and the app refuses to draw an interactive mesh with a spec version it does not know — it plays the server-rendered video instead. That is the honest failure mode.

Resolved

Resolved: the renderer is undecided; R3F is the incumbent; an ADR gates it. 02 §5.2 declared Filament picked, then withdrew it; 05 Stage 0 and 06 §2.3 both wrote their plans around React Three Fiber. No renderer is picked here. architecture/decisions/0004-3d-renderer.md decides it in the Stage 0 spike, before anyone ports MeshViewer.tsx.

Candidate The case for it The question that decides it
A. expo-gl + @react-three/fiber/native (incumbent) The look is proven in this exact stack; the generated spec is written for three.js; shaders and scene are OTA-updatable, which capture behaviour is not Does expo-gl still ship in SDK 57 and work on RN 0.86 new architecture? (No legacy arch to fall back to.) Animation advance and geometry parsing land on the JS thread
B. react-native-filament Native glTF/GLB loading, GPU skinning, a real PBR model, composites over a camera preview Does it build on RN 0.86 new-arch with react-native-worklets@0.10.1 present, and does it pull react-native-worklets-core? If it does, either the VisionCamera rejection is void or B is. No demonstrated RN 0.86 support
C. react-native-wgpu + three.js WebGPURenderer Same scene graph and same generated spec as A, on a maintained Shopify module alongside Skia Is three's WebGPU backend stable for skinned/vertex-animated meshes, and does it build on RN 0.86 new-arch?
~~RealityKit~~ — iOS-only; ARView is heavyweight to embed three times; faint additive ribbons need CustomMaterial + Metal, at which point we are writing a renderer anyway
~~SceneKit~~ — On Apple's deprecation path, iOS-only, gives nothing A does not

The gate, in this order: (1) build before benchmark — pnpm add <candidate> && npx expo prebuild --clean && pnpm ios:device on RN 0.86 with reanimated 4.5.1 and worklets 0.10.1 present, reporting whether react-native-worklets-core appears in the lockfile; a candidate that does not build is out before anything is measured. (2) On the survivors, stand up one <ShotScene> on an iPhone 12 and measure sustained fps on a 51-frame vertex-animated GLB, memory with 20 ribbons + 1 mesh resident, and time from require() to first frame. (3) Pass = ≥ 55 fps, ≤ 180 MB, ≤ 400 ms.

The insurance is real only because every candidate satisfies the same contract:

// src/render/ShotScene.tsx — renderer-agnostic. Written first, implemented once, replaceable.
export type BodyPart = 'leg_kicking' | 'leg_support' | 'hip' | 'trunk' | 'arm' | 'shoulder';

export interface ShotSceneProps {
  /** Every shot in the session, in the GOAL frame G. Faint. */
  trajectories: { shotId: string; points: Vec3[]; confidence: number; basins?: Vec3[][] }[];
  selectedShotId: string | null;
  /** Zero, one or MORE bodies. Two is the comparison case; three is shot+best+keeper. */
  meshes?: { glbUri: string; timeS: number; alignFrame: number;   // each clip's OWN contact frame
             style: 'solid' | 'ghost'; tint?: string; opacity: number }[];
  playing: boolean;
  goal: { widthM: number; heightM: number };
  shootingZone?: Vec2[];
  camera: { mode: 'orbit' | 'locked'; lockedTo?: CameraPose };
  onSelect(shotId: string): void;
  onSelectBodyPart(part: BodyPart): void;      // the two-way mesh↔stat binding
}

meshes is a list, and each entry carries its own alignFrame, because the compare view aligns two bodies on each clip's own kick frame, not frame 0 — a single optional mesh with one timeS cannot represent that, and the keeper is a third entry.

Renderer-neutral obligations, stated as behaviour rather than as an API:

# Obligation Why
1 Light ratios 3.6 : 3.2 : 1.1 are preserved; match the ratio, then scale until the sampled body values reproduce on a reference frame Absolute intensity is renderer-specific; the look is the ratio
2 Translucent bodies write no depth, and the opaque skeleton draws before them With depth-write on, a translucent body punches holes in whatever is behind it
3 The 22 joints and 21 bones are two batched draws, not 43 43 draw calls per frame at 60 fps on an iPhone 12 is the frame budget on its own
4 No post-process bloom The share video has none
5 One frame loop drives every animation on the canvas The pending pulse and the idle orbit must not be separate loops

Trajectory rendering — a tube, not a 1 px line, because a hairline disappears at depth and cannot be tapped. Radii are metres in G:

State Radius Material
faint (accumulated, unselected) 0.018 m unlit #8A93A6, opacity 0.22, no depth write
selected 0.032 m lit #FF8C26 + emissive #FF801F — the bone orange. The ball trail and the skeleton share one accent; that is the visual claim that they are the same measurement
lowConfidence (speed_confidence < 0.5) 0.018 m as above but dashed, 0.12 m on / 0.08 m off
basinAmbiguous 0.024 m each every entry in basin_solutions[] at selected colour, opacity 0.9 / n, as a band
pending (ball lane not settled) — launch-point ring only. No arc — we have not measured one
refused — launch-point ring + × glyph
personalBest overlay 0.024 m compareReference, opacity 0.45

Plus a 1 m-scale arrival marker sphere (r = 0.045 m) on the goal plane — the arc bundle is noisy, the impact cluster is not — a ground shadow (projection at 0.010 m, 10 % opacity; depth on a phone is ambiguous and the shadow resolves it), and an apex tick only when claims.apex === true.

basin_ambiguous is the normal case, not an edge case. Draw every surviving basin, put an "N possible arcs" chip on the sheet row and near the apex region, suppress the apex tick entirely, and render the arrival marker at half opacity with no grid-cell label — the endpoint is as uncertain as the arc. Never silently pick rank 1.

Selection: ray-pick against an invisible companion tube at r = 0.09 m (≈ 44 pt at typical framing), never against the visible 0.018 m one. If two hits fall within 0.15 m along the ray, do not pick — expand the side sheet filtered to those shots. Guessing which of two overlapping arcs the user meant is how a shot map becomes untrustworthy.

The map dot's fill encodes the mode, not the refusal — in Quick Mode every shot is ESTIMATED, so a hollow ring is the normal Quick mark and cannot also mean "refused":

Fill Extra channel
VERIFIED solid primary —
ESTIMATED / BAND_ONLY hollow ring, primary stroke Estimated pill on the sheet row
refused hollow ring, mutedForeground stroke × glyph inside the ring + the reason on the row
failed hollow ring, destructive stroke ! glyph + retry on the row

The mesh camera is the hero-render camera — distance 3.7 m, height 1.05, target y 0.95, fovY 41° from RENDER_SPEC, with orbit clamps 2–8 m and damping 0.1, a reset-view button, and a contact-frame detent on the scrubber with a haptic (the impact moment is the whole point). Two-finger pan is disabled — it fights the scroll gesture.

Resolved

Resolved: the mesh camera setup. Three existed (MeshViewer 2–8 m, OverlapMeshViewer 2–12 m damping 0.1, and 02 §5.3(b)'s CAM_POS [0,0.9,4] with a 1.2–6 m pinch clamp). 06 §4.3's hero-render pose + MeshViewer's clamps win, because this view's whole job is to match the share video the user just watched. 02 §5.3(b) is superseded.

Video replay: v1 plays the server-rendered mp4. The pipeline already produces ball_overlay.mp4 (tracked ball + reprojected fit on the raw frames), so v1 is expo-video on that artifact — zero client compositing, zero drift risk between overlay and video. The in-app interactive overlay is v1.1, gated on a projection unit test against the server's own trajectory_overlay.png. When it ships, the palette is the artifact's, taken from ball.py's own drawing code — fitted polyline #FFC800 at 2 px, tracked detection #00FF00, interpolated point #FF0000, conf < 0.5 point #00A0FF — because that two-colour confidence encoding is the only place the user can see where we lost the ball. Draw only up to the current playhead: a fully-drawn arc over a ball that has not been kicked yet reads as fake. Playback speeds are 0.25× / 0.5× / 1× / 2×, default 0.5×.

Resolved

Resolved: playback speeds. 01 §3.5 says three options; 06 §4.2 says four, which is what OverlapMeshViewer actually ships and what the compare screen will show. Four wins, so the two screens do not disagree.

Resolved: RadarChart does not carry over. 01 §5.2 lists it as carrying over "per-item only"; 06 §1.6 drops it. Dropped. A radar's readable output is the polygon's area — it is the composite presentation of per-item data, which is exactly what the brief forbids — and its colours and greys are hard-coded off-system. Replaced by <RegionBars> (per-region horizontal bars, no headline similarity %).

Mesh asset format: GLB with baked vertex animation, no rig. Not PLY. The HeroShot web contract is per-frame PLY at 397,723 bytes/frame (10,475 verts) — ~20 MB for 1.7 s (source: ~/Desktop/programming/opencap-pages/assets/mesh3d/). Mobile does not consume that, and no PLY loader goes in the app.

Variant What ships to the phone Size (DERIVED) Licence exposure
SMPL-X params + on-device skinning The body model ~50 KB + a 15 MB model in the binary Rejected — putting MPI's research-only model in the app binary is the landmine
Skinned GLB + Draco Decimated mesh + joint hierarchy + LBS weights (i.e. the rig) ~60 KB base + ~15 KB animation + ~45 KB glTF/inverse-bind/padding ≈ 120 KB Open — a skinned GLB carries the SMPL-X joint hierarchy and skinning weights to every user's phone
Baked vertex animation, no rig ← picked, provisionally Decimated mesh + per-frame vertex positions. No joints, no weights, no parent list ~60 KB base + 51 frames × 4,200 verts × 3 × float16 quantised ≈ 1.0–1.5 MB Lowest — but not zero, and not decided here
Resolved

Resolved: the mesh artifact and its budget. 03 §1.3 S7, 05 §4.4 and 06 §1.6 all specify a skinned GLB with ≤ 64 joints at ≤ 400 KB; 02 §5.5 picks the rig-free baked-vertex-animation variant at ≤ 1.5 MB. 02 §5.5 wins: it is the newer decision, it is the variant with the least licence exposure and the most topology-independence (which the design system requires anyway), and the 400 KB budget was set against a variant we are not shipping. Actions: 04 §4.4's storage row changes 2.5 MB → 1.5 MB (dropping the steady-state per-shot total from ≈ 16 MB to ≈ 15 MB); 03 §1.3 S7's contract block drops "one skin, ≤ 64 joints"; the CI budget check lives in be-vision, on the export stage. The open legal question stands and is routed to D1: does a decimated, rig-free, vertex-animated mesh derived from an SMPL-X fit require a Meshcapade licence to distribute? If Meshcapade is licensed, switch to the skinned variant and the budget drops 10×.

artifact kind: "mesh_glb"     (fetched via ShotDetailOut.pose.mesh_url, signed 10 min)
  glTF 2.0 binary, KHR_draco_mesh_compression on the base mesh
  units: metres · up: +Y · forward: +Z
  frame:  GOAL frame G — the same frame as trajectory.points_3d
  origin: pelvis ground-projection, expressed in G
  ONE mesh, no skin, no joints; animation as morph targets / vertex streams, 30 fps
  clip window: [contact − 1.0 s, contact + 0.7 s]
  extras: { "contactTimeS": 1.348, "sourceFps": 52.64, "personId": "0",
            "renderSpecVersion": "1", "bodyModel": "smplx" }
  budget: <= 1.5 MB, enforced in be-vision CI

The producer stage does not exist and must be scheduled. pipeline/export.py writes PLYs and nothing produces the GLB. The bake is not a format flip — it needs decimation (20,908 → ~8,000 tris) and quantised per-frame vertex streams — and it must read MotionResult.verts directly, not the stride-8 PLYs (stride 8 is ~6.6 fps and the animation clip is 30 fps). Budget unmeasured; allow 2–4 s and verify in the first Stage 2 run.

Which preset is the default, answered by use rather than by taste (render one style per job):

Surface Preset Why
ShotDetailScreen / MeshInspectorScreen mesh Opaque reads shape and posture best; that screen is about your form
Impact-moment freeze hologram The emissive skeleton makes a joint angle legible; the ghost body keeps the pose readable
ShotCompareScreen mesh for reference at opacity 1.0, mesh for mine at 0.55, tinted compareReference / compareMine Exactly the existing overlap-viewer behaviour
Keeper challenge (P1) hologram for the keeper The keeper is a simulation, not a measurement. A ghost body is honest signalling; a solid one implies we filmed him
Share video (P1) hologram It is the one that looks like nothing else on a feed
Anonymised media fallback mesh No face, hair or skin — already the privacy answer

⇒ mesh is the default job style; hologram is requested explicitly by the surfaces above.

Nothing in the visual language may depend on SMPL-X topology. Materials, lights, camera and floor are topology-free. The two things that are not: SMPLX_PARENTS and the range(22) joint loop → move behind a SkeletonTopology value in contracts/render.py ("smplx-22"), so a swap is a data change; and classifyBodyPart's y/x spatial buckets → already topology-free (it buckets on normalised world position, not joint indices). Leave it crude; do not "improve" it into a joint-weight lookup that a body-model swap would break.

Accessibility is structural, not a pass at the end. The 3D view is never the only path: every trajectory tappable on the map has a row in the side sheet; every body part tappable on the mesh has a chip in the stat grid, and the binding runs both ways so the grid is a complete alternative interface to the mesh; the 2D goal-mouth placement grid is a complete alternative to the map. The 3D surface is accessibilityElementsHidden and shots are announced through the sheet — "Shot 3, 23 to 48 kilometres per hour, Estimated, arrived top left" — the band is the value in speech exactly as it is in type, never a point value with the band as an aside. Lane transitions are announced ("Shot 4 trajectory ready"), never signalled by a visual-only pulse. Colour is never the only channel. Reduce Motion turns the draw-on into a cross-fade, the pulse static, and the idle orbit off.

4.5 Storage

Supabase Postgres for rows, Supabase Storage for bytes. All buckets private; nothing is public.

Bucket Contents Retention Typical size
clips-shot The per-shot passthrough clip the client uploads. Original samples, never re-encoded 14 d, overridden to clip_retained_until for a validation_sample 26 MB (rank 2)
clips-playback Worker's H.264 720p re-encode for in-app replay and share. Never analysed 365 d 4 MB
overlays ball_overlay.mp4, ball_strip.png (5-tile QA strip) 30 d 3.5 MB
mesh mesh.glb (Draco), params.npz, optional frames.ply.zip glb 180 d · ply 7 d · npz forever 1.5 / 1.5 / 20 MB
renders Hero render, share asset — produced on tap only 30 d 5 MB
fields Setup reference frame + goal-corner thumbnail life of the profile 0.4 MB
jk-content JK lesson videos, tip cuts, record clips. Not user-scoped life of the catalogue 40–400 MB/lesson 
{bucket}/{user_id}/{session_id}/{shot_id}/{name}.{ext}

clips-shot/8f2c…/3a91…/b7d0…/shot.mov
clips-playback/8f2c…/3a91…/b7d0…/playback.mp4
overlays/8f2c…/3a91…/b7d0…/ball_overlay.mp4
mesh/8f2c…/3a91…/b7d0…/mesh.glb
renders/8f2c…/3a91…/b7d0…/hero_hologram.mp4
fields/8f2c…/_field/{field_profile_id}/setup.jpg
jk-content/_catalog/{lesson_id}/lesson.mp4

user_id is the first path segment for three operational reasons that actually hold: retention is a per-prefix lifecycle rule and quota accounting is a prefix listing rather than a table scan; GDPR deletion is DELETE by prefix in every bucket, which is what the deletion sweep records; and a mis-signed URL cannot span two users. (A Storage RLS policy is worth creating as a backstop against a future direct-client path, but it is not the reason for the layout — every read and write here goes through a be-api-minted signed URL or the worker's service-role key, and both bypass RLS.)

Resolved

Resolved: upload granularity, transport and route. 02 §6.1 and 04 §1.2 D1 each deferred to the other and ended up inverted. They agree on granularity — one artifact per shot — and the byte arithmetic settles it beyond argument: a 20-shot, 8-minute session is ~3.6 GB as a continuous tape versus ~520 MB as 20 × 26 MB ring extractions (DERIVED at the ladder's rank-2 bitrate; source: 02-mobile-architecture.md §6.1), and the temporal crop costs nothing because the ring has to exist anyway. They disagree on transport (TUS vs S3-style multipart) and module (analysis vs training). 04's transport and route win: POST /v1/analysis/uploads, presigned per-part PUT, 8 MiB parts, with the server-side upload_session / upload_part rows, the 15-minute abandoned-upload reaper and the resume/checksum semantics 04 already specifies. 02's actual requirement — an iOS background upload task needs an upload-from-file body and must resume at a chunk boundary — is satisfied by a presigned part PUT exactly as it is by a TUS PATCH. Actions: 02 §6.3's pseudo-flow changes from TUS to presigned parts; 04 §4.3's window becomes [T − 1.0, T + 2.5] and its size row becomes 26 MB at rank 2; clip_window_s keeps its job (trimming the IDR-aligned lead-in and S1's exact window); clips-crop/clips-playback stays server-derived and the server never analyses it.

POST   /v1/analysis/uploads                       UploadInitIn  -> UploadTicketOut   201
PUT    <presigned part url>                       × ceil(bytes / 8 MiB)   ≈ 4 parts for a 26 MB shot
POST   /v1/analysis/uploads/{upload_id}/complete  UploadDoneIn  -> UploadDoneOut     202
GET    /v1/analysis/uploads/{upload_id}                         -> UploadStatusOut   (resume)
DELETE /v1/analysis/uploads/{upload_id}                         -> 204
  • Resume is GET …/{upload_id} → committed part numbers plus freshly-signed URLs for the rest. The client never guesses an offset. Backoff 1 s, 2 s, 4 s, 8 s, 30 s, then park; a BGProcessingTask (com.stryke.app.upload-drain) retries parked uploads on charge + Wi-Fi.
  • Checksum. sha256 is declared on init and verified on the assembled object. Mismatch ⇒ 409 upload_checksum_mismatch, the object and artifact row are deleted, shot.status returns to pending_upload. A truncated clip must never reach the pipeline, because it will produce a confident wrong answer rather than an error. UploadDoneOut returns the server's digest so the client can compare before deleting its copy — the local clip is never deleted until the server has acknowledged the object.
  • max_bytes = 256 MB per in-app shot object; a photo_library import goes through the same route with a 2 GB cap.
  • Known iOS behaviours neither side should pretend away: background upload tasks do not guarantee ordering, and iOS will not run them while suspended on cellular under Low Data Mode. Both surface as "waiting for signal" rows, not as errors.

The sidecar rides with each shot clip as its own artifact under the same output_prefix, and AnalysisJob gains exactly one optional field, sidecar_uri: str | None = None:

{
  "shotId": "…", "sessionId": "…", "sequence": 7,
  "trigger": { "source": "audio+vision", "confidence": 0.9, "deltaFrames": 2 },
  "window":  { "requestedS": [-1.0, 2.5], "trimLeadS": 0.184, "clipDurationS": 3.68 },
  "timebase": { "nominalFps": 60.0, "cfr": true, "droppedFrames": 0 },
  "camera":  { "intrinsicsSource": "in_app_capture", "formatRank": 2,
               "exposureUs": 1000, "iso": 640, "heightM": 1.62, "tiltDeg": 9.4,
               "azimuthDeg": 68.0 },                   // 90 = side-on
  "motion":  { "maxBackgroundShiftPx": 2.1, "attitudeDriftDeg": 0.21,
               "framingState": "ok", "thermalStateAtCapture": "fair" },
  "zone":    { "checked": true, "violation": false },
  "decimation": { "ballLane": [0,1,2,…], "poseLane": [0,8,16,…] },
  "frames":  [ { "i": 0, "ptsNs": 1234567, "fx": 2846.1, "fy": 2846.1,
                 "cx": 1919.5, "cy": 1079.5 }, … ]     // one entry per frame
}

It is not a CONTRACT_VERSION bump (an optional field with a default is not breaking), and it is not a third mechanism for something with two: CaptureMetadata and CameraSpec are inlined on the job and carry the constant and summary values; the sidecar exists for the one thing that cannot be inlined — per-frame K and PTS for up to 840 frames. Say that reason in the field's docstring so nobody later "simplifies" it back into CameraSpec.

Size budget — per shot, by artifact age (a hero render exists only for the ~20 % of shots the user taps, so it is amortised at 1 MB):

Age Resident artifacts MB
0–14 d shot clip 26 · playback 4 · overlay+strip 3.5 · glb 1.5 · npz 1.5 · render 1 37.5
14–30 d playback 4 · overlay+strip 3.5 · glb 1.5 · npz 1.5 · render 1 11.5
30–180 d playback 4 · glb 1.5 · npz 1.5 7.0
180–365 d playback 4 · npz 1.5 5.5
> 365 d npz 1.5 (kept forever) 1.5 
∫ size · d(age) = 14·37.5 + 16·11.5 + 150·7.0 + 185·5.5
                = 525 + 184 + 1050 + 1017.5  =  2 776.5 MB·days per (shot/day)

A user shooting 20 shots a week is 2.86 shots/day ⇒ rolling resident ≈ 7.9 GB, bounded, plus ≈ 1.5 GB/year of params.npz, which is kept forever and does grow. Soft cap: 12 GB per user. On exceeding it, evict oldest-first in this order and tell the user: clips-shot → overlays → renders → clips-playback → mesh/*.glb. Never evict params.npz (it is the only thing from which a mesh can be regenerated) and never evict an artifact referenced by a validation_sample.

Signed URLs: client upload part — minted by be-api, 30 min. Worker download — minted at dispatch and embedded in AnalysisJob.video_uri, 2 h (must exceed the job deadline plus one retry). Worker upload — service-role key inside the worker. Client playback — minted per request on GET /v1/training/shots/{id}, 10 min, never stored, never logged. Never return a raw storage path to the client.

On-device storage. Shot media does not live in Documents/, which iCloud backs up by default — three sessions a week would push ~1.5 GB/week into a user's iCloud backup, enough to break a 5 GB free tier inside a week and something App Review flags. So: ring segments in Library/Caches/stryke/ring/ (purgeable by the OS, which is correct — a ring is disposable); shot media, sidecars and thumbnails in Library/Application Support/stryke/sessions/… with isExcludedFromBackupKey = true set on the stryke directory at first launch. Total device media cap 4 GB, LRU over shots the server has acknowledged; a shot in pending_upload / uploading / upload_failed is never evicted, and when the cap cannot be met without evicting one, recording stops.

4.6 Coaching engine (multi-shot detection)

This is the normative statistic. The Insight / InsightShot tables are the right shape and are unchanged; core/insights.py's emit() and impact_score implement exactly what is below.

Candidate set — eight metrics: trunk_lean_deg, knee_deg, support_knee_deg, hip_deg, arm_angle_deg, shoulder_hip_rotation_deg, approach_speed_kmh, peak_foot_speed_ms. A metric is a candidate only if the upper 95 % CI bound of σ_repeat / σ_bio is ≤ 0.5 (§8.4). Metrics that fail are dropped from the candidate set entirely — not shown with a caveat. If the study drops metrics the multiplicity correction gets easier, so M is read from the study result, not hard-coded at 8.

The eligible-shot window. A shot enters only if pose_confidence ≥ 0.60 and c_pose == 1.0 (contact agreed within 17 ms — the angles are all evaluated at contact, so a contact error is a systematic angle error) and it shares a comparison cell with the others. A Quick-mode shot passes all of these, which is the point. Mixing a 25 m free kick with an 8 m dribble finish and calling the difference a "pattern" is the confound that makes coaching wrong; the cell keys are the same ones the ranking cohort uses, from the same key function.

The statistic. The baseline must be computed from shots outside the window being tested:

window   = the player's last n eligible shots in the cell
baseline = the 30 eligible shots immediately preceding that window (never overlapping)
μ_m, σ_user,m = that baseline's mean and SD

z_im    = (x_im − μ_m) / σ_user,m
flag_im = 1  iff  |z_im| ≥ 1.5                       # a real deviation from their own norm
                 and |x_im − μ_m| ≥ 2 · σ_repeat,m   # bigger than the measurement can fake
                 and sign(z_im) is the "limiting" direction for m

k = Σ flag_im  over the window;  one-sided exact binomial vs p₀ = 0.30
emit(m) iff  n ≥ 7  and  p < α/M (Holm)  and  no active insight is inside its 3-shot cooldown

Two corrections that were manufacturing false claims: the denominator is the player's own SD, not the population σ_bio (which is measured from one kicker × 20 shots with ~16 % relative SE — using it for every player is not defensible; the fallback below 15 baseline shots is σ_bio with baseline_source: "population" stamped on the insight, never silently); and μ_m no longer contains the tested shots (an overlapping baseline makes each z_im measured against a mean containing x_im, so the flags are negatively correlated and the binomial's independence assumption fails).

p₀ = 0.30 is a deliberate conservative choice, not an unmeasurable quantity. Under normality P(|z| ≥ 1.5) = 0.1336, and the second condition only lowers it; 0.30 is ≈ 2.2× that implied null, absorbing the non-normality of joint-angle distributions and the uncertainty in σ_user. Task V-2 may raise it from population data; it may not lower it below 0.1336.

Multiplicity: Holm at α = 0.05 across the candidate set. With 8 candidates tested at a nominal 0.05, the chance at least one fires by luck is 1 − (1 − 0.0288)^8 ≈ 21 % — about one player in five would get a fabricated pattern, and the ranking rule then selects the largest effect among them, which is winner's curse on top. The primary metric must clear α/8 = 0.00625:

n eligible k needed one-sided p Note
7 6 0.0038 the brief's headline case, and it clears Holm
7 5 0.0288 clears a naive 0.05, fails Holm — do not surface
10 8 0.0016
12 9 0.0017
15 10 0.0037
7 4 0.126 not significant under any correction

That "6 of 7" survives Holm and "5 of 7" does not is a real and useful result: the brief's own example sits exactly at the edge of what the data supports, and the copy must not be loosened past it.

Minimum n = 7 eligible shots in the cell is a hard floor. With n = 5 even 5-of-5 gives p = 0.0024 (which would clear Holm) but the sample cannot distinguish a habit from one bad set, and the brief is explicit that one-off movements get no feedback.

Resolved

Resolved: the coaching gate. 01 §3.6, 06 §4.5 and 04 §3.10 each carried a different rule ("fewer than 5 analysed shots"; window_shots >= 6, matching >= 5, matching/window >= 0.60, confidence >= 0.5, |effect| > 1.0 SD, no multiplicity control; and no cell requirement at all). 03 §9 wins verbatim — the cell requirement is the substantive part, and no amount of statistics repairs a bad window. Actions: 06 §4.5's empty-state copy becomes "fewer than 7 eligible shots in this cell" with a 7-dot progress row; 04's emit() and impact_score are rewritten to the block above; the eligibility floor reads pose_confidence ≥ 0.60, not analysis_confidence ≥ 0.55.

The single-issue ranking rule. Multiple metrics can be significant at once; only one is ever shown, chosen from those that survived Holm:

impact_m = w_m × median(|z_im| for flagged i) × matching_ratio_m,   matching_ratio_m = k_m / n
  • w_m is the metric's outcome weight — the standardised coefficient of metric m in a per-player regression of the shot outcome on the posture metrics. unmeasured; until the Run 2 set exists it is seeded from the JK reference model and outcome_weight_source: 'jk_reference' carries that to the copy layer, which has to actually say it.
  • matching_ratio replaces an earlier p_significance_bonus: multiplying by a function of the p-value ranks winners by how surprising they were, which is precisely the winner's-curse amplifier. k/n is a property of the player, not of the test.
  • Ties break toward the larger k/n, then toward the metric closer to the kinetic-chain root (trunk before arm).
  • The reported effect is shrunk. Because the shown metric is the maximum of several, its raw effect is biased upward. Report the direction and the count in copy — "leaned back in 6 of 7", never "leaned back 14 % more than usual". This costs nothing in the UI and removes the inflation entirely.
  • Cooldown: once surfaced, it is the only issue for the next 3 eligible shots, then re-tested. No new issue is raised inside the cooldown even if it becomes significant.
  • Verification: after the cooldown, report the paired change in the flagged metric and in the outcome (speed / placement / repeat consistency), with the same eligibility filter. If the metric moved but the outcome did not, say exactly that — it is the honest and more useful result.

The engine emits {metric, k, n, p, p_holm_threshold, direction, baseline_source, impact_rank, cooldown_remaining} and the copy layer turns that into the sentence. The engine never emits prose, and there is no free-text body column on insight — copy lives in the i18n catalogue keyed by template_key, which is how the wording rule stays guaranteed and translatable.

4.7 Rankings

The cohort key is a single string so it can be a plain indexed column and a cache key:

v1|dist:{band}|goal:{preset}|shot:{type}|foot:{foot}|age:{band}
v1|dist:16-18|goal:full|shot:free_kick|foot:right|age:18-29
Dimension Values Source
dist 2 m bins: 4-6, 6-8, … 28-30, 30+ shot.distance_m, only when distance_source='fitted'
goal full, futsal, mini, custom-{w}x{h} field_profile.goal_preset
shot free_kick, dribble, first_touch shot.shot_type
foot right, left shot.foot only when foot_source='pose'
age u13, u16, u18, 18-29, 30-39, 40+ leaderboard_eligibility.age_band_snapshot

Built by core/cohort.py::cohort_key(dims) — pure, no I/O, unit-tested on band boundaries (a 20.0 m shot must land in exactly one band, deterministically, forever). The same module exports cell_key(field_profile_id, foot, shot_type, distance_bin) for the coaching window, and both use the same distance_bin(). Bumping the v1 prefix retires the whole distribution set when a band definition changes.

Resolved

Resolved: distance binning. 04 §9.1 originally used 7 bands 3–7 m wide; 03 §2.1/§9.2 uses 2 m bins and calls the coaching cell and the ranking cohort "the same key function". 2 m wins — the wider bands were a volume optimisation applied to only one of the two consumers, which silently de-synchronises them. The parent-cohort ladder below is what recovers the volume the thin bins cost.

conf is not a cohort dimension. A confidence tier is a property of the measurement, not of the population, so binning by it would compare a user's good clips only against other people's good clips and quietly inflate everyone. Confidence is a gate, applied before a shot enters any distribution.

Three dimensions are self-reported by default, and all three are closed:

  1. Foot. shot.foot starts as a hint; the pose lane's kicking_foot overwrites it and sets foot_source='pose'. A shot whose foot_source='declared' is not ranked — a left-footed cohort is thinner, so declaring left would otherwise be a one-field percentile boost.
  2. Age. age_band_snapshot is written onto the eligibility row at evaluation, exactly as calibration_hash is. Deriving it live meant one PATCH /accounts/me retroactively re-cohorted a user's entire history into u13. Editing birth_year writes a profile_change_log row, applies to future shots only, and moves history only after an admin sets applied_to_history.
  3. Distance. A ranked shot's distance_m is hypot(X, Z) of the fitted launch point in G, not the zone centroid, and launch_in_zone tests that point against the profile's shot_zone. Without both, a user registers a 23–30 m field with a wide dribble_zone — the BRIEF's own description of that zone — and banks every 10 m shot as a measured 23–30 m one.

The minimum-sample rule. is_published is true only when all three hold: sample_users >= 30 (distinct verified users — thirty is the point at which one person's session cannot visibly move a decile; ten would make one enthusiast 10 % of the population), sample_shots >= 200, and sample_shots_90d >= 50 (the cohort is alive, not a fossil from launch week).

Until publication the API returns percentile: null and the app falls back exactly as BRIEF §7 requires: personal best → JK benchmark → STRYKE verified benchmark. Optionally it also falls back to a parent cohort with is_fallback_cohort: true so the app can label it. The ladder is fixed and stops at the first published ancestor:

1. widen dist 2 m → 6 m    v1|dist:12-18|goal:full|shot:free_kick|foot:right|age:18-29
2. drop age                 v1|dist:12-18|goal:full|shot:free_kick|foot:right|age:all
3. drop foot                v1|dist:12-18|goal:full|shot:free_kick|foot:all|age:all
4. widen dist to 12 m       v1|dist:12-24|goal:full|shot:free_kick|foot:all|age:all

goal and shot are never dropped: comparing a futsal goal against a full one, or a free kick against a first touch, is not a wider cohort — it is a different question. A fallback percentile is never presented as the percentile.

The metric sample is an explicit view, because the metrics live in three tables and one of them is a window function:

CREATE VIEW metric_sample AS
  SELECT s.id AS shot_id, s.user_id, e.cohort_key, s.captured_at,
         'ball_speed_kmh'::text AS metric_key, s.ball_speed_kmh AS value
    FROM shot s JOIN leaderboard_eligibility e ON e.shot_id = s.id
   WHERE s.ball_speed_kmh IS NOT NULL
  UNION ALL
  SELECT s.id, s.user_id, e.cohort_key, s.captured_at, 'placement_error_m',
         sqrt(power(s.arrival_x_m - z.centre_x, 2) + power(s.arrival_y_m - z.centre_y, 2))
    FROM shot s JOIN leaderboard_eligibility e ON e.shot_id = s.id
    JOIN goal_zone_centre z ON z.zone = s.target_zone          -- a 9-row lookup table in core
   WHERE s.target_zone IS NOT NULL AND s.arrival_x_m IS NOT NULL
  UNION ALL
  SELECT s.id, s.user_id, e.cohort_key, s.captured_at, 'peak_foot_speed_ms', p.peak_foot_speed_ms
    FROM shot s JOIN leaderboard_eligibility e ON e.shot_id = s.id JOIN pose p ON p.shot_id = s.id
   WHERE p.peak_foot_speed_ms IS NOT NULL
  UNION ALL
  SELECT s.id, s.user_id, e.cohort_key, s.captured_at, 'approach_speed_kmh', p.approach_speed_kmh
    FROM shot s JOIN leaderboard_eligibility e ON e.shot_id = s.id JOIN pose p ON p.shot_id = s.id
   WHERE p.approach_speed_kmh IS NOT NULL;

placement_error_m requires a declared target_zone — without a declared target there is no error to measure, and inventing one (distance to the nearest cell centre) would score a wild shot that happened to land near a corner as accurate. repeat_consistency is not in this view: it is a rolling SD of arrival position over a user's last 10 eligible shots, so every shot would contribute ten heavily overlapping windows and the per-user cap would not bound their influence. It is computed as one value per (user, cohort) per refresh, cap 1 by construction.

-- nightly: rankings/service.py::recompute_cohort(cohort_key, metric_key, direction)
WITH capped AS (
  SELECT m.shot_id, m.user_id, m.value, m.captured_at,
         ROW_NUMBER() OVER (
           PARTITION BY m.user_id
           ORDER BY CASE WHEN :direction = 'high' THEN m.value END DESC NULLS LAST,
                    CASE WHEN :direction = 'low'  THEN m.value END ASC  NULLS LAST) AS rn
    FROM metric_sample m
    JOIN leaderboard_eligibility e ON e.shot_id = m.shot_id
   WHERE m.cohort_key = :cohort_key AND m.metric_key = :metric_key
     AND e.is_verified AND e.invalidated_at IS NULL AND e.state = 'evaluated'
     AND m.metric_key = ANY (SELECT jsonb_array_elements_text(e.eligible_metrics))
     AND m.captured_at > now() - interval '365 days')
SELECT percentile_disc(ARRAY(SELECT generate_series(1,99) / 100.0))
         WITHIN GROUP (ORDER BY value)                          AS breaks,
       count(*)                                                 AS sample_shots,
       count(DISTINCT user_id)                                  AS sample_users,
       count(*) FILTER (WHERE captured_at > now() - interval '90 days') AS sample_shots_90d
  FROM capped WHERE rn <= :per_user_cap;      -- 10 for per-shot metrics, 1 for repeat_consistency

The ROW_NUMBER() is direction-parameterised: a plain ORDER BY value DESC kept each user's worst ten for low-direction metrics such as placement_error_m, so every low-direction distribution was built from the population's worst attempts. sample_shots_90d is actually computed — it was required by is_published and returned by nothing, so no cohort could ever have published.

The 99 breakpoints go into cohort_percentile.breaks, and a shot's percentile is never stored — it is a binary search over breaks at read time with direction deciding which end is "top". So it cannot go stale, cannot disagree between two screens, and costs nothing to recompute.

Refresh: nightly full at 03:00 UTC over every cohort with sample_shots > 0; hourly incremental over cohort_dirty rows with new_shots >= 50; invalidation sweep within 5 minutes for cohorts touched by a camera_moved, a suspension or an upheld dispute. Cohorts below the threshold are still computed (so you can watch them approach it) and return is_published=false.

Per-item, never overall:

metric_key Definition direction
ball_speed_kmh shot.ball_speed_kmh, point value only high
placement_error_m distance in G from the arrival point to the centre of the declared target cell low
repeat_consistency SD of arrival position over the user's last 10 eligible shots in the cohort; needs ≥ 10 or returns null low
peak_foot_speed_ms pose.peak_foot_speed_ms high
approach_speed_kmh pose.approach_speed_kmh high

A consistency figure computed from three shots is noise wearing a percentage sign; the ≥ 10 floor is not negotiable.

Anti-gaming:

Control Mechanism
Verified only leaderboard_eligibility.is_verified is the single gate, and it is a CHECK constraint plus a trigger, not a convention inside an evaluator
Confidence gate speed_confidence >= 0.75; speed additionally needs claim_point_speed and band_width_pct <= 20. Placement and consistency can still rank, because trajectory shape is one tier more robust than the speed number
Pose gate c_pose == 1.0. It is a hard multiplicand, so a contact_disagreed shot scores 0 and cannot rank however clean the ball fit looked
In-app capture only capture_source = 'in_app' AND capture.original_file is a hard precondition inside the trigger; otherwise not_in_app_capture, and SCREEN_RECORDING_SUSPECTED adds its own reason
Launch-point enforcement launch_in_zone must be true; distance must be fitted
Foot / age provenance foot_source = 'pose'; age_band_snapshot written once
Camera-moved invalidation A recalibrate_required verdict writes from_shot_sequence; every shot at or after it gets invalidated_reason='camera_moved', invalidated_at=now(), cohorts dirty, and personal bests set by them superseded on the next sweep
Duplicate bytes artifact UNIQUE (user_id, sha256) WHERE kind='clip_shot', plus a nightly cross-user perceptual-hash check
Duplicate kick shot UNIQUE (user_id, window_fingerprint) — re-exporting the same window produces different bytes and the same fingerprint
Per-user cap Best 10 shots per user per cohort, direction-aware; repeat_consistency uses 1. One person's 400-shot afternoon cannot become the cohort
Field edits An edit recomputes calibration_hash; shots keep the hash they were taken under. A distance edit > 1 m invalidates that field's ranked shots outright
Stale profiles Staleness caps a shot at ESTIMATED, and re-verifying does not promote it
Suspension Drops all of that user's shots from is_verified on the next sweep; documented exit
Rate 60 shots/hour/user_id, 3 sessions/hour/device_install_id. Above the limit shots are stored and analysed normally and the eligibility row is written state='review_pending' — a state that holds a deferred evaluation, which the rule previously assumed and did not have

The launch gate: rankings ship dark. is_published stays false for every cohort, and the API returns reasons=[{"code":"rankings_not_launched"}], until the radar validation runs and every row of §8.5 passes. Everything else — personal best, JK comparison, insights, keeper probability, the shot map — ships before that gate, because none of them needs an absolute scale.

05 Calibration design (LiDAR and non-LiDAR)

The goal of the ritual is a field_profile whose closure_residual proves it, plus a per-session camera pose in G. It runs once per venue; subsequent sessions only re-verify framing.

The ritual is the same walk on both device classes. What differs is only how the four goal corners get their 3D positions. LiDAR is an aid, never a requirement — which the physics forces anyway.

5.1 The walk (Ranked mode)

Step UX What is measured Failure signal Confirmation shown
1. Goal size Preset picker: full 7.32×2.44 · futsal 3×2 · mini · custom nothing — it is declared custom width outside 2–8 m → block "Full size · 7.32 × 2.44 m" chip, visible for the whole session
2. Ground lock "Point at the grass for 2 seconds" ARKit horizontal plane → ground_plane_w; LiDAR densifies it no plane after 6 s, or plane normal > 8° from gravity the plane shades in green
3. Left post Walk to the left post, stand at its base, tap At the post post_bases_w.left = ARKit camera position projected onto the ground plane, then displaced 0.30 m along the camera-forward axis projected into that plane ARKit tracking state ≠ .normal a pin drops at the post
4. Right post Same at the right post post_bases_w.right as above second pin; the goal line draws between them
5. Closure check automatic ‖right − left‖ vs the declared width §5.3 "Measured 7.29 m vs 7.32 m — 0.4 % off. Good."
6. Ball spot Walk to the shooting spot, tap Ball goes here launch point in W → shot_distance_m spot outside 3–40 m of the goal centre the distance is stated in metres, and is user-correctable
7. Camera placement Walk to the camera spot, mount the phone, tap Camera here camera_placement in G; azimuth_off_axis_deg computed §5.5 a live framing overlay with the pass/fail checks
8. Corner taps in-frame Tap the four goal corners on the live preview 2D corner pixels → the PnP anchor the fit uses — v1: the raw taps draw the goal quad, unsnapped
9. Save "Save this field" field_profile persisted — zones drawn on the pitch view

Why steps 3–5 and not "just tap the posts from where you stand": the tap-only path resolves the goal from a single view, and single-view PnP is degenerate when the goal is fronto-parallel — four coplanar corners cannot separate focal from depth. detect_goal already detects that case (post-length asymmetry < 4 % and crossbar angle < 2.5°) and downgrades to scale-only; both shipped example runs hit it. The walk gives an independent, metric, non-degenerate measurement of the same rectangle, and the disagreement between the two is the calibration residual we report.

Why we do not rely on automatic goal detection, and why step 8 cannot snap in v1. detect_goal thresholds white as (V > 160 & S < 60). On the one real goal clip in the repo (a sunset shot) that mask is 47 % of the frame — the sky — HoughLinesP returns 628 lines of which exactly one is vertical, and the detector returns goal_not_detected on every probed frame but one, where it returns a quad 20 m too near. The A/B harness had to hand-measure the corners. So user taps replace the detector in v1, and with them the "corner tap > 25 px from a detected white vertical" rejection — both assume a detector that does not work on our one real goal clip. Re-enable snapping only after a detector passes a stated acceptance on real goal clips; until then the detector stays as the Quick-mode best-effort and as the per-frame tracker for a quad the user already placed.

5.2 LiDAR vs non-LiDAR

LiDAR devices — three additions, all in the near field:

  1. Ground plane and camera height from sceneDepth + the ARMeshAnchor floor rather than a feature-plane fit. Expected: sub-centimetre on camera_placement.height_m at the ritual's 0.3–2 m working range, versus a plane-fit height that is unmeasured.
  2. Post-base snap. At steps 3–4 the user is standing at the post, so it is within 0.3–1.5 m — inside LiDAR range. A depth raycast gives the post base directly, removing the 0.30 m hold-offset assumption and its posture-dependent error.
  3. Ball-spot elevation on a sloped pitch — the difference between a trajectory that lands on the ground and one that lands 0.4 m under it.

What LiDAR does not do: measure the goal from the camera position, measure the ball in flight, or improve the speed number directly. Apple's published range is ~5 m; a goal filmed from a legal camera position is 10–35 m away, so LiDAR contributes nothing at all to measuring the goal at range. If UX copy implies otherwise, it is wrong — and no screen, no state and no cohort key branches on whether the device has LiDAR.

Non-LiDAR devices — nothing changes in the walk. Three differences:

  1. Post bases come from the ARKit camera position at the tap, projected onto the ground plane and displaced 0.30 m along camera-forward. That offset is a fixed constant and it is posture-dependent — a tall user at arm's length and a short user at chest height do not have the same offset. Measure the distribution and put its SD into the confidence inputs; do not assume it is zero.
  2. The goal rectangle is the ruler, not the walk. The declared 7.32 × 2.44 is exact by definition, and four in-frame corner taps + pinned intrinsics give depth without PnP solving focal:

Z_goal = f_px · W_goal / (w_px / cos ψ) # w_px = pixel distance between post bases # ψ = goal yaw relative to the image plane ∂Z/Z = ∂w/w ⊕ ∂f/f ⊕ tan(ψ)·∂ψ

UNMEASURED — engineering estimate. Do not quote a number from this table to anyone.

Two reasons it is not a measurement. (a) There is no measured tap σ. The obvious candidate — TT3D's corner MAE of 2.39 ± 1.47 px (source: …/23-stryke-heroshoot/ball-accuracy-upgrade.md T2.3) — is an algorithm's calibration error, not a human finger's, and step 8 is a user tap on a live preview that v1 cannot even snap. (b) The fronto-parallel relation does not hold in our operating regime: the framing gate deliberately coaches the camera off the shot axis, so the goal is normally yawed and w_px carries a cos ψ foreshortening. At ψ = 45° the tan ψ coefficient is 1.0, so a 2° yaw error is a 3.5 % depth error on its own.

Goal distance w_px (ψ=45°) σ_tap = 2.4 px (algorithmic, optimistic) σ_tap = 8 px σ_tap = 12 px
15 m 491 3.6 % 4.2 % 4.9 %
25 m 295 3.7 % 5.2 % 6.7 %
35 m 211 3.8 % 6.4 % 8.8 %

(σ_w/w_px ⊕ tan ψ · σ_ψ with σ_w = √2·σ_tap and σ_ψ = 2°; the yaw term alone is a flat 3.5 %.)

Read the columns, not the cells. At the optimistic σ the yaw term dominates at every distance and nothing below ~3.5 % is reachable without solving ψ. At a realistic finger σ the tap term overtakes it past ~20 m and the total runs 4–9 %. It is still a large win over the ball's own diameter ruler, which the literature measures at 24–28 % relative depth error — a factor of roughly 3–8×, not an order of magnitude (source: …/23-stryke-heroshoot/ball-accuracy-upgrade.md §1, citing Van Zandycke & De Vleeschouwer CVPRW 2022 Table 2). The one way to collapse the yaw term is not a better tap: it is non-coplanar PnPf, which solves ψ, focal and depth jointly. 3. IMU gravity supplies the Y_G axis — ARKit's world frame is already gravity-aligned, so this is free; the check is that the ground-plane normal agrees with gravity to within 8°.

5.3 The closure check — the honest core of the ritual

Two independent measurements of the same rectangle exist after the walk: declared (the preset the user picked) and walked (‖post_bases_w.right − left‖, pure ARKit VIO), plus an optional taps measurement. closure_width_err_pct = |walked − declared| / declared is a direct observable of VIO scale error, obtained for free, and it is the gate:

Residual closure_grade Verdict UX
≤ 2 % good accept "Measured 7.29 m vs 7.32 m — good."
2–5 % degraded accept, degraded c_cal = 0.6; "Close enough to analyse, not close enough to rank."
> 5 % rejected reject "That walk drifted. Do it again in better light, and don't cover the camera." Re-register

The crossbar gets the same check against the declared 2.44 m, with a looser 4 % / 8 % gate because height taps are 2D-only (nobody walks to the crossbar) and the residual folds in the corner-tap error.

The 2 % and 5 % cuts are initial engineering values, UNVALIDATED. Task C-2 measures the actual distribution over 20 registrations across 3 venues and 2 device classes, reported separately for LiDAR and non-LiDAR, and the cuts move to the measured 90th and 99th percentiles. Do not let them calcify as if measured.

Resolved

Resolved: does the closure budget close on a non-LiDAR device? 05 row 15 says a non-LiDAR device reaches Verified "exactly like a LiDAR one". That is the design intent, not a measured fact, and the identity exposes a real risk: over a 7.32 m walk the error is VIO drift + 2 × post-base localisation error, and on a non-LiDAR phone the hold-offset term alone at a plausible per-post σ of 0.10 m contributes √2 × 0.10 = 0.14 m ≈ 1.9 % — the entire 2 % budget, with nothing left for VIO drift. If C-2 lands there, A10 is unreachable without LiDAR and LiDAR quietly becomes a requirement, which BRIEF §6 forbids.
