            raise ValueError("speed_kmh present without claims.point_speed")
        if self.apex_m is not None and not self.claims.apex:
            raise ValueError("apex_m present without claims.apex")
        if self.elevation_deg is not None and not self.claims.elevation:
            raise ValueError("elevation_deg present without claims.elevation")
        if self.claims.spin_magnitude:
            raise ValueError("spin magnitude is never claimable")
        # a CLAIM with nothing behind it — the identical bug, inverted
        if self.claims.point_speed and self.speed_kmh is None:
            raise ValueError("claims.point_speed with no speed_kmh")
        if self.claims.apex and self.apex_m is None:
            raise ValueError("claims.apex with no apex_m")
        if self.speed_kmh is None and not self.speed_refusal_reason:
            raise ValueError("a refused point speed must say why")
        # structural
        if self.frame != "G" and self.goal_crossing is not None:
            raise ValueError("a goal crossing requires the goal frame")
        if self.railed_parameters and self.confidence_inputs.c_rail != 0.0:
            raise ValueError("a railed solve must report c_rail = 0")
        return self

speed_band_kmh needs no guard — it is a required, non-optional tuple, so "the band is always present" is enforced by the type system. fit.central_estimate_kmh exists for debugging and A/B and must never be shown to a user; it is permitted as an input to offline validation analysis (refusal precision), and that permission is written down here so nobody has to guess.

class PoseMetrics(BaseModel):
    """Flat by design — this is the KickProfile shape the comparison screens read. Every angle is
    `float | None` so a missing value renders an em-dash, never 0 degrees."""
    kicking_foot: str | None = None; contact_frame: int | None = None; contact_time_s: float | None = None
    peak_foot_speed_ms / peak_ankle_speed_ms / peak_knee_speed_ms: float | None = None
    approach_speed_kmh: float | None = None
    knee_deg / support_knee_deg / hip_deg / arm_angle_deg: float | None = None
    trunk_lean_deg / shoulder_hip_rotation_deg: float | None = None
    confidence: float | None = None
    # No overall score. Per-item only.

class PoseResult(BaseModel):
    model_name: str; model_version: str
    body_model: str                             # smplx | mhr | gemx
    model_license_class: str                    # noncommercial | commercial
    mesh_fps: float; mesh_frame_count: int
    kicker_person_index: int = 0; kicker_fully_in_frame: bool
    metrics: PoseMetrics = PoseMetrics()
    confidence_inputs: ConfidenceTerms = ConfidenceTerms()   # fills c_pose, c_kp, c_subj

class ArtifactRef(BaseModel):
    kind: ArtifactKind; object_key: str; content_type: str; bytes: int; sha256: str | None = None
    shareable: bool = True     # false when model_license_class == 'noncommercial'

class AnalysisResult(BaseModel):
    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid")
    contract_version: str = CONTRACT_VERSION
    job_id: UUID; shot_id: UUID; session_id: UUID; lane: Lane; attempt: int = 1
    status: JobStatus; pipeline_version: str; worker_id: str; cold_start: bool = False
    render_spec_version: str | None = None      # the app refuses to draw a spec version it does not know
    ball: BallFlight | None = None              # lane=ball,   status=succeeded
    pose: PoseResult | None = None              # lane=pose,   status=succeeded
    render: RenderResult | None = None          # lane=render, status=succeeded
    artifacts: list[ArtifactRef] = Field(default_factory=list)
    quality_flags: list[QualityFlag] = Field(default_factory=list)
    refusal_reasons: list[RefusalReason] = Field(default_factory=list)
    failure_class: FailureClass | None = None; failure_code: str | None = None; error: str | None = None
    timings_ms: dict[str, float] = Field(default_factory=dict)
    started_at: datetime | None = None; completed_at: datetime | None = None

    @model_validator(mode="after")
    def _payload_matches_status(self) -> "AnalysisResult":
        if self.status is JobStatus.SUCCEEDED:
            payload = {Lane.BALL: self.ball, Lane.POSE: self.pose, Lane.RENDER: self.render}[self.lane]
            if payload is None: raise ValueError(f"succeeded {self.lane} result carries no payload")
        if self.status is JobStatus.REFUSED and not self.refusal_reasons:
            raise ValueError("a refusal must say why")
        if self.status is JobStatus.FAILED and self.failure_class is None:
            raise ValueError("a failure must carry a failure_class so retry policy can decide")
        return self

AnalysisJobV1.upgrade() takes lane as a required argument (hard-coding POSE would silently discard the ball half of every drained job; be-api's drain loop calls it twice and enqueues both), and calibration_uri is deliberately dropped with a log line, because v2's equivalent is FieldGeometry, which be-api rebuilds from the profile. AnalysisResultV1.upgrade() is not optional: without it a v1 worker draining in-flight jobs during the migration returns a body AnalysisResult cannot parse (extra="forbid" plus four new required fields), and every in-flight job dies at the callback.

FakeVisionPipeline must emit the full v2 result, deterministically by shot_id hash: 70 % succeeded-with-band-only, 15 % succeeded-with-point-value, 10 % refused (TOO_FEW_DETECTIONS), 5 % failed (INFRASTRUCTURE). That distribution is roughly what the field produces and it is the only way the app's empty and refusal states get built at all. Guard: settings.vision_pipeline == "fake" must raise at startup when ENV == prod — the default stays fake so every laptop keeps working, and only prod refuses it.

7.3 The job state machine

be-api owns every transition. The worker writes none. (An earlier design had the worker claim jobs with UPDATE analysis_job SET state='dispatched' WHERE state='queued', which would give be-vision a Postgres connection and knowledge of a table's name, columns and state values — and it cannot work for the pose lane at all, because Modal functions are invoked, not polled, so there is nothing to claim.)

State Meaning Written by
queued Job row written, waiting for the dispatcher API, on upload complete
dispatched API has invoked the worker; the lease timer starts here API, at .spawn()
running First JobProgress received API, on the events callback
retry_wait Infra failure; backoff timer running API reaper
cancelling Cancel requested on an in-flight job API, on cancel
succeeded Result stored, claims applied API, on result callback
refused Ran fine, declined to produce numbers API, on result callback
failed Terminal failure (input, contract, or attempts exhausted) API
cancelled Worker acknowledged the cancel, or the lease expired after one API 
                       ┌──────────────────────── manual retry (cap 2) ─────────┐
                       │                                                       │
pending_upload ──upload complete──▶ queued ──API .spawn(), lease 60 s──▶ dispatched
                                      ▲                                    │
                                      │                          first progress
                                      │                                    ▼
                                      │                                 running
                                      │                                    │
              backoff (30s/2m/8m ±20%)│         ┌──────────────────────────┼──────────────────┐
                                      │         │                          │                  │
                                  retry_wait ◀──┤ result(failed,           │ result           │ result
                                                │  class=infrastructure)   │ (succeeded)      │ (refused)
                                                │                          ▼                  ▼
dispatched|running ──lease/heartbeat expired──▶ retry_wait              succeeded          refused
                                                                        [terminal]        [terminal]
running ──result(failed, class=input|contract)──▶ failed [terminal]
queued|dispatched|running ──deadline_at passed (reaper)──▶ failed(code=deadline_exceeded)
attempt > max_attempts ──▶ failed(code=retries_exhausted)

queued|retry_wait ──cancel──▶ cancelled [terminal]
dispatched|running ──cancel──▶ cancelling ──worker ack | lease expiry──▶ cancelled [terminal]

failed | refused ──manual retry──▶ queued, attempt += 1, manual_retries += 1

Three things that are explicit and were previously missing:

  1. POST /analysis/jobs/{id}/retry has a transition. failed and refused are terminal for the automatic policy only; the UI's retry button needs a path, capped at manual_retries <= 2. Because attempt is in the idempotency key, a retry creates a genuinely new job instead of returning the failed one with 200. A retry of a refused job returns 409 retry_would_be_deterministic unless the operator passes force=true — re-running deterministic code on the same bytes produces the same answer, so only a pipeline-version change makes it worth doing, and that already re-enqueues by key.
  2. Cancel works on in-flight jobs. DELETE /training/shots/{id} and session abandon can fire while a job is dispatched|running; be-api calls the provider's cancel with provider_call_id and soft-deletes the shot. A result callback for a job not in dispatched|running|cancelling is a no-op returning 409 job_not_accepting_results, so a container that finishes after the shot is gone cannot insert a trajectory row against a vanished FK.
  3. needs_recalibration has an exit (§5.6).

Reaper: one asyncio task, tick every 15 s, SELECT … FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED, handling lease expiry, heartbeat staleness, deadlines and backoff wake-ups. The dispatcher is another, tick every 2 s.

Timeouts and retries:

Knob Value Why
Claim lease 60 s If a worker dies between dispatch and first progress
Heartbeat interval 10 s From the worker, on the events endpoint
Heartbeat staleness 30 s Three missed beats. (90 s would be nine.)
Job deadline, ball 120 s 4× the ~30 s budget
Job deadline, pose 600 s Cold container ~120 s + 82 s measured motion + headroom
Job deadline, render 300 s 117 s measured cold for two styles; we render one
Max automatic attempts 3
Max manual retries 2
Backoff 30 s → 2 min → 8 min, ±20 % jitter Cold GPU capacity comes back on minutes, not seconds
Retried classes infrastructure only An input refusal refuses identically on attempt 2; retrying wastes a GPU minute and lies to the user
Resolved

Resolved: the job knobs. 05 row 24 quotes pose 480 s and heartbeat staleness 90 s. 04's values above win — it owns the ledger, and 600 s is sized against the measured cold path (~120 s container + ~82 s motion), which 480 s is not. Likewise 05 #12's flat 180 s analysis timeout is below the measured cold p90 and would fire on every first shot of the day: it becomes conditional — 180 s when warmup.gpu_pool_warm is true, 300 s when it is false.

7.4 Two lanes, one shot — how shot.status is derived

A shot spawns two jobs at upload-complete (ball, pose) and one more on demand (render, when the user opens the mesh inspector or taps share).

Lane Stages (STAGE_COUNT) Hardware
ball 5: ingest → stabilize → track_ball → detect_contact → fit_3d CPU
pose 3: preprocess → motion → kinematics GPU
render 2: render → export GPU, on tap

The app's four-step tracker is a display grouping over these, not a second truth: step 1 Ball tracking ← ball stabilize, track_ball; step 2 Shot detection ← ball detect_contact, fit_3d; step 3 Body reconstruction ← pose preprocess, motion, kinematics; step 4 Report ← be-api's settle (confidence, eligibility, insights). The shot-map screen renders it as two progress rows, not one chain, because a serial four-stage list tells the user body reconstruction must finish before the report, which inverts the actual ordering:

Trajectory   ●━━●━━●━━○      Ingest · Track ball · Contact · 3D fit
3D body      ●━━○  ○  ○      Motion · Kinematics · (Render · Export are on tap)

The full derivation, written by exactly one function, training.service.recompute_shot_status:

ball lane pose lane shot.status What the app shows
no clip yet no clip yet pending_upload Sheet row, "waiting"
upload in progress — uploading Progress row
upload complete, jobs not yet written — uploaded "Queued"
queued/retry_wait queued/retry_wait queued Stage chip at step 0
dispatched/running any non-terminal analysing Stage chip + progress
succeeded non-terminal partial Arc drawn on the shot map. Mesh tile = skeleton placeholder. Confidence chip reads "pending" — c_pose is not in yet
succeeded succeeded ready Everything
succeeded refused ready Numbers + arc; mesh tile: "no body analysis for this shot" + the reason
succeeded failed/cancelled ready Numbers + arc; mesh tile: "3D replay unavailable — retry"
refused succeeded ready Pose carries the shot: mesh, posture, impact frame, no ball numbers, no arc
refused refused/failed/cancelled refused "We couldn't measure this one" + the ordered fixable reasons
failed/cancelled succeeded ready As above, plus a retry affordance on the ball lane
failed failed/refused/cancelled failed Retry button; the clip is retained
any any invalidated Overrides everything when invalidated_reason IS NOT NULL

Do not show a confidence number during partial. The trajectory lands without the GPU; speed_confidence does not, because c_pose is a hard multiplicand.

7.5 The serving grade

VERIFIED / ESTIMATED / BAND_ONLY / REFUSED are grades, not states, derived at serve time:

def grade(shot, eligibility) -> str:
    if shot.status in {"refused", "invalidated"}:  return "REFUSED"
    if eligibility.is_verified:                    return "VERIFIED"
    if not shot.claim_point_speed:                 return "BAND_ONLY"
    return "ESTIMATED"

Modelling grade as derived rather than stored is deliberate: a stored grade and a stored eligibility row can disagree, and then two screens show two answers.

Client-vocabulary mapping — one server enum, four projections:

Server (stored) Grade axis Device Chip
pending_upload — captured SEALED
uploading — uploading UPLOADING
uploaded / queued — uploaded / queued QUEUED
analysing — analysing ANALYZING
partial — partial PARTIAL
ready VERIFIED / ESTIMATED analysed READY
ready BAND_ONLY analysed LOW_CONFIDENCE
refused REFUSED refused REFUSED
failed — failed_upload / failed FAILED
invalidated REFUSED — INVALIDATED
— — unconfirmed / discarded (device-only, no server row)

04's refused status and 05's REFUSED grade are different things that share a word: the first means the job declined; the second means this quantity has no number. refused is a first-class success state, not an error — the shot has a video, it may have a shape, it just has no number, and the UI treats it as a shot.

There is no /v1/rankings/leaderboard. The screen is Later, no board is defined, and a route with no response model, no backing table and no anti-abuse story is worse than a missing one. It lands with the section that owns it.

GET /analysis/jobs/{job_id} changed meaning. The scaffold returned AnalysisResultOut{result: …}; v1 returns JobOut — job state, no result. That is a different resource, not a body that grew. Results come from GET /v1/training/shots/{shot_id}.

POST /v1/challenges/keeper is synchronous 201, not 202. save_probability is closed-form arithmetic on the stored trajectory (goal-crossing point, time of flight, keeper reaction time and reach), so there is nothing to queue: there is no keeper lane, no analysis_job.lane value for it, and no renderer for a simulated mp4 — which made state='running' unreachable.

The load-bearing response shapes:

class SpeedOut(BaseModel):
    """The shape that makes false precision unrepresentable."""
    kmh: float | None                   # null unless claimed AND released
    band_kmh: tuple[float, float]       # ALWAYS present
    claimed: bool; reason: str | None; tier: str | None      # A | B | C | null
    per_anchor: dict[str, dict]         # goal / diameter / motion_plane

class ShotDetailOut(BaseModel):
    id; session_id; sequence; status; grade; mode
    labels: list[str]                   # ["estimated"] for Quick Mode
    shot_type; target_zone; foot; foot_source; distance_m; distance_source; launch_in_zone
    speed: SpeedOut
    placement: PlacementOut | None      # arrival_x_m/y_m + zone + on_target + error_m
    trajectory: TrajectoryOut | None    # frame, points_3d, renderable_on_map, apex_m (+reason),
                                        # elevation_deg, basin_ambiguous, basin_solutions,
                                        # railed_parameters, overlay_url
    pose: PoseOut | None                # mesh_url (signed 10 min), metrics, kicker_fully_in_frame,
                                        # shareable
    media: MediaOut                     # playback_url, thumbnail_url, hero_render_url (null until
                                        # a render job has run ON TAP), qa_strip_url, expires_at
    comparison: ComparisonOut           # personal best -> JK -> stryke_verified -> percentile
    eligibility: EligibilityOut         # is_verified, cohort, eligible_metrics, ordered reasons
    quality: QualityOut                 # speed_confidence, pose_confidence, ALL confidence_inputs
                                        # with their nulls visible, tier, flags, coaching_hints
    jobs: list[JobOut]                  # so the retry affordance has something to act on

class ShotMapOut(BaseModel):
    goal: dict; frame: str = "G"; origin: dict
    shots: list[ShotMapItem]            # ONLY trajectory.frame == 'G'
    unrenderable: list[UnrenderableShot]  # reason: refused | trajectory_frame_b | analysing | invalidated
    truncated: bool
    #   Either session_id or field_profile_id is REQUIRED — the scope is per FIELD PROFILE, not per
    #   field: calibration_hash includes the camera placement, and a second placement at the same
    #   pitch is a different profile and a different G-to-camera transform.

class ShotComparisonOut(BaseModel):
    left: ShotSummaryOut; right: ShotSummaryOut | None; right_kind: str
    comparable: bool                    # false when calibration_hash differs
    incomparable_reason: str | None
    deltas: list[MetricDelta]           # PER-METRIC. Never a composite similarity score.
    overlay_renderable: bool            # both trajectories in frame G with the same goal

class WarmupOut(BaseModel):
    gpu_pool_warm: bool
    expected_first_result_s: int        # 45 warm, 240 cold — reports the COLD figure when cold
    expected_arc_s: int                 # ball lane only: 20 warm, 35 cold

ShotQualityScreen renders QualityOut directly — the terms with their nulls visible is exactly the "make our uncertainty inspectable" surface, and it is why confidence_inputs is stored rather than recomputed.

Share enqueues a render job whose watermark be-api sets from shot.mode, and returns 409 share_blocked_by_licence when pose.model_license_class == 'noncommercial' and the requested style includes the mesh — the licence gate is enforced at the only place that mints a public asset.

JKRecordOut.speed_comparable is a server field computed from calibration_hash equality against the field_profile_id in the query, so the "matching conditions only" rule cannot be forgotten by one screen. Without a field_profile_id in the query it is always false. The shape overlay is always available; only the speed comparison is gated. POST /v1/jk/challenges returns 409 jk_conditions_do_not_match with matching_field_profile_ids in the problem detail, so the app can offer the right field instead of a generic error.

7.7 Idempotency, polling and push

analysis_job.idempotency_key = sha256(f"{shot_id}:{clip_sha256}:{lane}:{pipeline_version}:{attempt}"), UNIQUE. Submitting the same shot+lane+attempt twice returns the existing job with 200. A pipeline upgrade changes the key, so a re-analysis after a release is a new job — which is what you want. Client-side, POST /v1/training/sessions/{id}/shots and POST /v1/analysis/uploads accept an Idempotency-Key header (client UUID, stored 24 h). Anti-replay is two separate constraints, neither of which is the idempotency key: identical bytes (artifact) and the same kick re-exported (shot.window_fingerprint).

Three delivery mechanisms, in order of preference:

  1. SSE — GET /v1/analysis/stream?session_id=… while the session screen is foregrounded. One stream per session, carrying JobProgress frames and terminal shot.settled events for every shot. This is the primary path; twenty shots must not become twenty poll loops.
  2. Polling — GET /v1/training/shots/{shot_id} when SSE is unavailable. Cadence 2 s for the first 30 s, then 5 s, then 15 s after 3 min — but every response carries Retry-After and the client obeys the header rather than its own timer, which is how two backoff ladders stop being able to drift apart.
  3. APNs silent push — one per shot when the last lane settles while backgrounded. Payload carries shot_id and terminal state only, never results.

Rule: SSE and push are optimisations. Reconciling on GET /v1/training/sessions/{id} must always be sufficient, because it is the only path that works after a cold app start.

Offline is the normal case — this is a football pitch. Capture is fully local: ring, extraction, sidecar, SQLite row, thumbnail, zero requests. At session end shots go to pending_upload and the map shows every shot as a marker with "waiting for signal". When signal returns the drain wakes (reachability listener + BGProcessingTask) and uploads oldest-first at concurrency 2, with the currently-selected shot promoted to the head of the queue — concurrency does not change the total (it is bandwidth-bound) but it changes which shot finishes first. DERIVED at 26 MB/shot: LTE at 10 Mbit/s ≈ 21 s/shot ≈ 7 min for 20 shots; Wi-Fi at 50 Mbit/s ≈ 4 s/shot ≈ 1.4 min. That arithmetic is why M2c exists.

7.8 Problem Details catalogue

Codes marked reason appear as eligibility.reasons[].code or refusal_reasons[] rather than as an HTTP error — the same slug is used deliberately so one string search finds every surface a failure reaches.

Code HTTP Surface
