08 Accuracy validation methodology

Nobody in this field has published this. Across ~40 repos and papers spanning soccer, tennis, badminton, table tennis, basketball, cricket and golf, not one reports a launch-speed error for a monocular camera against radar (source: …/23-stryke-heroshoot/ball-accuracy-upgrade.md §5). If we build this set we have a number nobody else has — and, more importantly, we have the only thing that converts our tier tables from typography into measurement.

Nothing else in the CV roadmap starts before this. But only half of it can be step 0, and pretending otherwise was a real defect: the condition matrix has a "Ranked (ARKit + field profile)" level and in-app capture modes, and those are produced by later steps. A step-0 gate cannot require artifacts that steps 1 and 2 produce. So the protocol runs twice.

Run 1 — step 0 Run 2 — after the ritual ships
Code today's ball.py, with S4-1 (az bound) landed Ranked path: goal-frame fit, pinned pose + focal
Capture stock Camera app, pulled over USB in-app stryke-capture (AVAssetWriter, per-frame K, pinned CFR)
Calibration Quick only Ranked + Quick side by side
What it answers is today's pipeline falsifiable, and where does it actually break? may rankings launch?
Gates A3′, A5, A12, A13 — measured, reported, none of them blocks the next step A1–A13, all of them

8.1 Protocol A — speed, against a radar gun

Equipment (~$300, one purchase): Pocket Radar Ball Coach (PR1000-BC). Vendor spec ±1 mph; independently within 1 mph on 593 of 642 pitches vs a Stalker; validated against the Stalker research criterion for soccer kicks specifically at ICC ≥ 0.98, r ≥ 0.98, bias ≤ 3.19 km/h, SDC ≤ 7.70 km/h (Rico-González et al., J Sports Sci 2021, PMID 33377436 — cited in …/23-stryke-heroshoot/ball-accuracy-upgrade.md §5).

Mounting — this is the part that goes wrong. Place the radar behind the target, on the shot line, at roughly ball height, on a tripod, never handheld. A radar reads the velocity component along its own axis; cosine error destroys it exactly the way filming down-the-axis destroys us. The same device moved from behind the goal to in front of it collapsed from CV 4.0–5.4 % / ICC 0.945–0.958 to CV 7.4–15 % / ICC 0.134–0.693, with error magnitude 17.0–37.5 km/h (Sensors 2022, PMC9505007, n = 153 — cited in …/23-stryke-heroshoot/ball-accuracy-upgrade.md §5).

Attribution, once, because two acceptance rows rest on it: the CV 4.0–5.4 % / ICC 0.945–0.958 belongs to the criterion radar, the Stalker ATS II — it is that instrument's own repeatability on soccer kicks. The behind-vs-in-front collapse is the mounting lesson from the same study.

Kickers and volume: 5–8 people spanning ability so speeds cover 40–110 km/h. Biological spread is large — soccer SDC ≥ 8.6 km/h between kicks by the same player — so the design needs many kicks per person, not many people.

The design is one-factor-at-a-time off a fixed baseline, and here is the arithmetic. A full factorial over 3 angles × 4 modes × 2 mounts × 2 references × 2 calibrations × 2 lights × 2 devices is 768 cells; at ≥ 12 per cell that is 9,216 kicks. An earlier draft asked for "≥ 100 paired samples, ≥ 12 per condition cell" against that matrix and budgeted one afternoon — a 92× gap between two numbers in the same paragraph. Neither is achievable as stated, so the design is named explicitly instead. Every cell differs from the baseline in exactly one factor.

Run 1 baseline: side-on ~90° · 1080p240 (stock, USB-pulled) · tripod · goal in frame · Quick · bright daylight · LiDAR device.

Run 1 cell Deviation Kicks
R1-0 — (baseline) 12
R1-1, R1-2 angle → oblique ~45°, down-the-axis ~15° 24
R1-3 … R1-5 mode → 1080p60, 1080p30, 4K60 36
R1-6 mount → braced handheld 12
R1-7 reference → goal absent 12
R1-8 light → overcast/dusk 12
R1-9 device → non-LiDAR iPhone 12
Run 1 total 10 cells 120

Run 2 baseline: same framing, in-app capture + Ranked.

Run 2 cell Deviation Kicks
R2-0 — (Ranked baseline) 12
R2-1 calibration → Quick, same clip set 12
R2-2 capture → stock camera (isolates the in-app capture win) 12
R2-3 angle → oblique ~45° 12
R2-4 device → non-LiDAR (pairs with the closure risk) 12
R2-5 reference → goal absent 12
Run 2 total 6 cells 72

Plus 3 interaction cells, chosen because that is where the failures are expected to live: down-the-axis × goal-absent · dusk × braced handheld · retimed clip × otherwise-baseline (this one exists specifically to catch a known band-coverage miss). 12 each = 36.

Total: 228 paired kicks across 19 cells. Run 1 (120 + 36 = 156) is one long afternoon with 5–8 kickers; Run 2 (72) is a separate half-day, reusing the same tooling.

What this design cannot do, stated so nobody reads more into it: OFAT measures main effects only. Any interaction not in the three named cells is unmeasured. If a cell fails, the design cannot separate "that factor is bad" from "that factor is bad in combination with the baseline's other levels". A cell that fails badly earns a follow-up 2×2 around it; it does not earn a conclusion.

Capture hygiene: pull the original files over USB / Files, never AirDrop (source: …/23-stryke-heroshoot/ball-accuracy-upgrade.md T1.7) — AirDrop bakes slow motion and hands back a retimed 30 fps file, which is an 8× speed error. A genuine 240 fps iPhone file reports ~239.02 fps; the baked export shows a 30 fps container with a duplicate-frame signature, and assess_timebase already refuses on exactly that (avg_frame_rate 30 vs r_frame_rate 239.02). Deliberately produce a few AirDropped files as the fixture set for the retimed interaction cell.

Logging: one CSV row per kick, with the radar reading photographed or voice-logged at the instant of the kick so pairing cannot drift.

kick_id,run,cell_id,radar_kmh,kicker_id,phone_model,capture_mode,fps_container,angle_deg,mount,
goal_visible,mode,field_profile_id,light,magnus_flag,pipeline_sha,az_bound_deg,clip_path,notes

field_profile_id is empty for every Run 1 row — Run 1 is Quick-only by construction. run, cell_id, magnus_flag, az_bound_deg and pipeline_sha exist so a result can be attributed to a configuration later; a basin result quoted without its model setting is worthless.

Free bonus: for ~20 kicks film with two phones and triangulate. Radar cannot touch elevation, apex or azimuth, and apex is our worst-disagreeing quantity — two-phone triangulation is the only ground truth we can get for the arc.

Source Apex spread across basins
Prototype / pre-Tier-1 solve 0.94 m vs 1.71 m (27.1° vs 39.3°)
Reference clip, current code 0.14 … 0.71 m across 4 basins (lateral cross-check gives 1.70 m)

The current 5× spread makes the point at least as well as the prototype's 1.8×, and it is the one a builder will reproduce today.

8.2 Protocol B — placement, against physically measured goal coordinates

Radar validates speed. It says nothing about where the ball went, and placement is half the product — BRIEF's gate sentence names both halves: "speed and placement error must be validated against at least a radar gun and physically measured goal coordinates".

  1. Tape-measure the goal and confirm 7.32 × 2.44 (or record the actual dimensions — many training goals are not what they claim).
  2. Mark the mouth with a 3×3 grid: gaffer tape on the crossbar and posts at X = ±1.22 m and Y = 0.813, 1.627 m, plus a plumb line at X = 0. Photograph the marked goal square-on as the audit artifact.
  3. Mount a goal camera on a tripod behind and above the goal, 4K60, static, with all four corners plus the tape marks in frame. Its own homography from the four known corners converts any pixel on the goal plane into (X_G, Y_G).
  4. Truth for a kick = the ball's centre at the frame it crosses the goal plane, through that homography.

Truth error (DERIVED): corner localisation σ ≈ 2.4 px over a goal spanning ~1400 px in a 4K frame is 2.4/1400 × 7.32 m ≈ 0.013 m, and ball-centre localisation at that resolution is ~4 px ≈ 0.02 m. Truth is good to ~±0.05 m — 6× tighter than the acceptance threshold, which is what a ground truth needs to be. The naive alternative (a human calling the cell by eye) is good to ~±0.25 m and is a same-day sanity check, never the acceptance number.

Also measured on the same rig, for free: shot_distance_m against a tape measure from the ball spot to the goal centre (truth ±0.02 m), which validates the calibration walk end to end.

8.3 Error metrics — report all four, never just one

  1. Bland–Altman — mean bias (km/h and %) with 95 % limits of agreement. This is the primary result and the one that exposes a systematic scale error, which is what we expect to have.
  2. ICC(2,1) and Pearson r — ranking quality. We predict high ICC with a real bias: the Athla signature.
  3. CV % per condition cell.
  4. Band coverage — the fraction of radar readings falling inside our reported speed_band_kmh. This is the honesty metric. Coverage < 90 % means the band is a lie regardless of how good the point value looks; coverage 100 % with a 20 km/h-wide band means the band is useless. Report coverage and median band width, always together, and both against the radar reading, never against our own point value — speed_kmh is null exactly when the band is the product, so a self-referential definition is undefined on the population that matters.

We already have one measured coverage failure, and it is still open. The 2026-08-19 verification log records: "the band does NOT cover truth on a retimed clip ([111.0, 205.8] vs truth 54.0) — only the point-value refusal saves it" (source: …/23-stryke-heroshoot/README.md log 2026-08-19, item 5). A 2× miss on a known input class. That is why the interaction cell list includes a retimed / duplicate-frame cell explicitly, so the run is designed to catch this rather than average it into a 90-something-percent aggregate. If that cell's coverage is not reported separately, A5 has not been evaluated.

Two more metrics specific to our refusal design:

  1. Refusal precision — of the clips the pipeline refused a point value on, what fraction would in fact have been outside ±10 % of radar? A refusal rule that fires on everything is not a safety feature, it is a broken product. Computing it requires a value for a refused clip, which is what fit.central_estimate_kmh is: explicitly permitted for offline validation, explicitly forbidden in any user-facing surface. Write that permission into the report template so nobody re-litigates it per run.
  2. Claim soundness — of the clips where claims.point_speed == true, what fraction land within the acceptance threshold? This must be ≥ 95 % or the claims block is not doing its job.

8.4 Repeatability study (posture) — the missing prerequisite for coaching

The coaching statistic needs a measurement-noise floor σ_repeat per posture metric, and it does not exist. The study:

  • 1 kicker, 20 nominally identical shots (same spot, same technique, same session), filmed simultaneously from 3 camera positions that all pass the framing gate.
  • σ_repeat,m is the across-camera-position spread of metric m on the same physical kick — three independent views of one event, so the only thing varying is the measurement.
  • σ_bio,m is the across-shot spread at a fixed camera position.
  • Report per metric: σ_repeat, σ_bio, σ_repeat / σ_bio, and a 95 % CI on each σ. At n = 20 the relative SE of an SD is ~16 %, so a bare point estimate of σ_bio will be over-read. Any metric where the upper CI bound of σ_repeat / σ_bio exceeds 0.5 is not usable for coaching and is dropped from the candidate set — not flagged.
Resolved

Resolved: no same-clip replicate. 05 Stage 1 asks for each clip through the pipeline 3×. ball.py and metrics.py are deterministic — pure CPU, fixed lattice, no stated stochasticity — so that arm returns the same answer three times, contributes σ = 0, and dilutes the pooled σ_repeat toward zero. That would make the σ_repeat ≤ 0.5·σ_bio screen too easy to pass and the 2·σ_repeat flag gate too permissive: a measurement-noise floor manufactured out of determinism. If a future stage becomes stochastic, reinstate the replicate and name the stage in the run README.

Which σ does the coaching z-score divide by? Not this one. σ_bio here is one kicker's spread and is used only as the screen that decides which metrics are coachable at all. The z-score denominator is the player's own SD from their own baseline shots (§4.6).

8.5 Acceptance thresholds — the gate on the rankings launch

Rankings do not ship until every Run 2 row passes. Run 1's rows are measured and reported at step 0; none of them blocks the next step, because Run 1 characterises today's code rather than accepting it.

# Quantity Threshold Run Rationale
A1 Speed bias, Ranked cells |bias| ≤ 5 % and ≤ 4 km/h 2 The criterion's own validated bias vs Stalker is ≤ 3.19 km/h, so a floor tighter than that cannot be demonstrated with this criterion. 4 km/h = √(3.19² + 2.4²) — the criterion's bias in quadrature with a 2.4 km/h allowance of our own. A 3 km/h threshold would be below the ruler's own bias.
A2 Speed 95 % LoA, Ranked within ±12 % 2 One tier looser than the bias; anything tighter is not credible without a better criterion
A3 Speed CV %, per Ranked cell ≤ 5 % 2 The source's stated target and the depth-ruler stage's exit criterion. A relaxation to ≤ 8 % was proposed by assertion and is withdrawn; the staged fallback below is not a loosening of A3
A3′ Speed CV %, per Run-1 cell reported, not gated 1 Run 1 characterises today's Quick pipeline — report the number, rank the cells by it, do not gate
A4 ICC(2,1) ≥ 0.90 2 Ranking quality. Athla's 0.970 (tennis, with a metric corridor we do not have) is the reference point, not the target
A5 Band coverage vs radar ≥ 90 % overall AND ≥ 90 % in the retimed interaction cell 1 and 2 The honesty metric; the per-cell clause exists because of the measured 2× miss
A6 Median band width reported, derived from A2 + A5 — not independently gated 1 and 2 see below
A7 Placement RMSE ≤ 0.30 m 2 derived: a 3×3 mouth cell is 2.44 × 0.81 m, so 0.30 m is ~37 % of the cell short side — the largest error that still supports A8
A8 Placement 3×3 zone accuracy ≥ 80 % correct cell 2 What the percentile surface actually claims
A9 shot_distance_m bias ≤ 2 % vs tape 2 The cohort key; a wrong distance bin silently corrupts every percentile
A10 Calibration closure 90th pct width_err_pct ≤ 2 %, reported separately for LiDAR and non-LiDAR 2 The split is the open non-LiDAR risk (§5.3)
A11 Claim soundness ≥ 95 % of claims.point_speed == true clips within A1/A2 2 The whole gating design
A12 Refusal precision ≥ 50 % of refused clips would truly have been outside ±10 % 1 and 2 Refusals must be informative
A13 Detection yield ≥ 20 real detections on ≥ 70 % of the set 1 and 2 The cheapest early signal that the 2D stage is or is not the problem
Resolved

Resolved: A6 is derived, not set — the three band rows were not jointly satisfiable. A2 (LoA ±12 %), A5 (coverage ≥ 90 %) and an independent A6 (median band width ≤ 20 % of the point value, i.e. ±10 %) cannot co-exist. At zero bias, LoA ±12 % implies σ ≈ 6.1 %, and the coverage of a ±10 % band is P(|z| ≤ 1.63) = 89.6 % — failing A5 by construction, before any bias exists. The three only co-exist in a narrow window around bias ≈ 5 %, σ ≈ 3.6 %, i.e. the thresholds reward a biased estimator. So: fix A2 and A5 and let the band width be whatever they imply — a band that covers 90 % of a distribution with σ from A2 must be at least ±1.65σ. Report the achieved median width against that implied width. A band nobody can act on is still a product failure; it is a finding to escalate, not a row that silently trades against coverage.

A13 exists because detection yield is not settled. On the worst-case screen-recorded, panning, 54 %-duplicate-frame clip the three-tracker ensemble achieved 48 real detections — that is the number behind "don't replace this stage" (source: ~/Desktop/programming/stryke-pipeline/docs/BALL.md §Validation). But both example outputs committed in the repo sit at n_detections = 10 (source: …/out/unanchored/ball_flight.json, …/out/goal_anchored/ball_flight.json), below the ≥ 20 point-value floor and two above the hard refusal. A "don't touch this stage" instruction resting on a 48 from a different clip, while both shipped examples sit at 10, is not safe to act on. So the stage is not replaced for accuracy reasons yet — none of our six accuracy problems lives in 2D centre estimation — but yield becomes a measured acceptance row, and it decides whether WASB / TrackNetV3 / BlurBall (all MIT) get built in v2.

Never claim, at any point, from one phone: better than ±3 %. The criterion radar has CV 4.0–5.4 % on soccer kicks. A number tighter than the ruler is not a measurement.

Four outcomes are worth naming in advance, because each has a different consequence:

Measured result Verdict
A1–A13 all pass on a cell Rankings may launch on that cell only
A5 fails (coverage < 90 %) Stop. The band is wrong. Re-derive it from this dataset, not from the retracted tier tables. Do not launch rankings on any cell
A5 passes, A6's implied width is unusable The band is honest and useless. Widening is not a fix; ship session-relative ranking only and wait for the depth-ruler stage. This is the likely outcome today — the shipped band on the reference clip is [28.8, 76.6], 47.8 km/h wide
Speed rows fail, A7/A8 pass Placement percentiles may launch while speed stays dark. eligible_metrics is per-metric precisely so this is expressible without a special case

8.6 Report format

One directory per validation run, committed:

validation/YYYY-MM-DD-radar-run-N/
├── README.md          # conditions, equipment serials, weather, who kicked, what broke
├── kicks.csv          # the raw log + one column per pipeline output field
├── results.md         # the tables below, GENERATED — never hand-typed
├── bland_altman.png   # speed, all cells + one per Ranked/Quick
├── coverage.png       # band coverage vs band width, per cell
├── placement.png      # predicted vs truth (X_G, Y_G) with the 3x3 grid drawn
├── goal_truth/        # the marked-goal photographs + the goal-camera homography JSON
└── clips/             # original files, checksummed, NEVER re-encoded

# Radar validation RUN 1 (or 2) — YYYY-MM-DD
Pipeline commit: <sha>   Contract version: <n>   --magnus: <flag>   az bound: <±deg>
Capture: <stock | in-app>   Calibration: <quick | ranked>   N pairs: <n>

## 1. Verdict against §8.5, rows assigned to this run
| # | Quantity | Threshold | Measured | Pass |
(Run 1 reports A3′, A5, A12, A13. Run 2 reports A1–A13.)

## 2. Bland–Altman, all cells        bias, bias %, 95% LoA, n
## 3. Per OFAT cell (baseline first, one factor per row)
| cell_id | deviation | n | bias% | CV% | ICC | coverage | median band width | implied 1.65σ width |
## 4. Interaction cells
down-axis × goal-absent · dusk × handheld · RETIMED × baseline (coverage reported ALONE)
## 5. Placement       RMSE, 90th pct error, 3×3 zone confusion matrix
## 6. Refusals        n refused, grouped by reason string, refusal precision.
   NOTE: refusal precision uses fit.central_estimate_kmh. Offline validation ONLY.
## 7. Detection yield (A13)   histogram of n_detections; % ≥ 20; % < 8
## 8. What moved since the last run, and what did not

Cost. ~$300 for the radar plus ~$40 for the second tripod, gaffer tape and tape measure. Run 1 is one long afternoon (156 kicks including the interaction cells) plus ~2 days of tooling and analysis — "the cheapest and highest-value item in this entire document. Everything else is unfalsifiable without it." Run 2 is a separate half-day (72 kicks), reusing the same tooling. The placement rig adds an extra hour on the same afternoon.

Two feeders keep the corpus alive after the runs. A user dispute writes a shot_dispute row and inserts a validation_sample with source='dispute', truth_source='user_claim', and extends the clip's retention past the 14-day lifecycle — otherwise the evidence expires before anyone reviews it. A user claim is not ground truth and is excluded from every acceptance computation: between-kick SDC for one player is ≥ 8.6 km/h and players systematically over-estimate. A clip enters the truth set only after it is re-measured against radar or the goal-camera rig. More than 3 disputes in a week on one calibration_hash pages the validation lead.

09 Failure cases and recovery UX

The product's job is not to hide failure. It is to name failure, label the number honestly, and route the user to the one action that fixes it.

9.1 Recovery principles

Never silently degrade. Four rules, each enforceable in review:

  1. A tier or eligibility change is always announced. If a shot would have ranked and does not, the first fixable reason from eligibility.reasons is on the shot card, one tap from the number — not buried in a settings screen.
  2. A missing number is shown as missing, with its reason. speed_kmh == null renders as the band plus Why a range?, never as a blank, a dash, a zero, or a band midpoint. A missing angle renders an em-dash with a "not measured" tint, never 0°.
  3. No fallback path produces a plausible-looking number. The fake pipeline is a dev tool, not a degradation strategy.
  4. The recovery sheet names exactly one action. The coaching rule — recommend only the single highest-impact issue — applies to error recovery too. eligibility.reasons is already ordered by fixability, so the sort is a read, not a computation. The rest go under More tips.

Copy rules: say what happened, then say the one thing to do — two lines. Never blame the user's technique for a capture failure ("We lost the ball", not "you shot too fast"). Never use error, failed or invalid for a measurement outcome. We are describing our own measurement's limits, not the user's.

9.2 The labelling contract — four axes, four independent renderings

Merging any two of these is how a wrong number reaches a screen.

Surface mode = ranked mode = quick
Mode badge Verified pill, ShieldCheck Estimated pill, Gauge. Never styled as a warning
Shot-map dot solid, brand accent hollow ring
Share asset numbers + Verified on {field} numbers + a visible Estimated watermark, server-applied
JK comparison full head-to-head only under a matching field profile trajectory overlay only; no speed comparison
Surface claim_point_speed = true and released otherwise
Speed card 41 km/h, band on tap 29–77 km/h at the same type size — the band is the value. Never a point value with a "±" whisper
Apex / elevation rows rendered when claims.apex / claims.elevation row hidden, apex tick not drawn
Spin Topspin, curling left — qualitative chip same. There is no RPM anywhere, ever
Surface tier ∈ {A,B} and speed_confidence ≥ 0.75 tier ∈ {C,D}
Trajectory ribbon solid dashed
Coaching window enters at pose_confidence ≥ 0.60 excluded
Keeper challenge (P1) enabled disabled with an explanatory chip
Surface is_verified = true = false
Rankings included, per eligible_metrics excluded; the row is visible in Your shots with the first fixable reason
Percentile row Speed: top 18 % — only after the rankings gate the benchmark ladder instead: personal best → JK → STRYKE verified

No accuracy-tier badge ships. The ±5–8 % / ±10–15 % / ±20–30 % tiers were retracted as "an engineering estimate wearing the typography of a measurement". The confidence tier chip drives rendering decisions and is a P0 feature; it is never printed as a ± percentage next to a speed. Those are two different chips: (b) ships, (c) never does.

9.3 The failure catalogue

Exact copy is in bold: title line, then body line. Threshold provenance is tagged on every number.

A. Capture — detected on the phone (owner: CaptureScreen; all eleven are triggerable on a pitch in one session)

# Failure Detected by Copy Recovery Disposition
1 Camera moved — framing lost framing: IMU attitude drift > 1.0° or visual background shift > 12 px (full-res, measured on the 960×540 buffer) or \|userAcceleration\| > 0.35 g shock [DERIVED — §5.6]. Rates: IMU 50 Hz, visual 20 Hz. Backstop: camera_pan_compensated [MEASURED] Camera moved. / Put the phone back and we'll re-check the framing — takes about 5 seconds.
KO: 카메라가 움직였어요. / 원래 자리에 다시 놓아 주세요. 5초면 프레이밍을 다시 확인할 수 있어요.		 Recording auto-pauses and the app pushes the re-verify screen, which ghosts the reference frame at 40 % with the saved goal quad outlined. Re-lock when the shift is < 3 px sustained 1.5 s → same field_profile_id, new camera_placement_id, same cohort. No match in 60 s or a quad > 2 % off → full re-registration and a cohort-key change. The blocking sheet also offers Continue as Estimated, which sets Quick semantics on the remaining shots Because capture auto-pauses, shots "after the move, before re-verify" should not exist. Any that do take the invalidation, not a downgrade: invalidated_reason='camera_moved', invalidated_at=now(), cohorts dirty, and personal bests set by them superseded on the next sweep
2 Camera drift — amber framing: drift 0.5–1.0° or shift 3–12 px [DERIVED] Non-modal. HUD bar turns amber; end-of-session line: The phone moved a little. / Brace it or use a tripod — it costs you a tighter number, not the shot. Nothing blocks; motion.maxBackgroundShiftPx goes in the sidecar Captured and uploaded with the drift recorded. c_frame takes a WARN multiplier, so the tier typically drops out of A. Stays in personal history
3 False-positive detection (clap, whistle, another player, ball into the net, a passing car) detect: audio onset fired, vision did not confirm → unconfirmed [SPEC] Tray chip only, no sheet: Was that a shot? / We heard something but didn't see the ball leave. Keep it or bin it. The clip is cut and kept in the tray, badged amber, Keep / Discard. Never auto-uploads Device state unconfirmed; nothing exists server-side until the user keeps it. This is the dominant failure mode of the app's single most important interaction decision, so it gets a first-class affordance, not a toast
4 Missed shot — quiet strike, vision also missed neither channel fired Persistent button, always on screen: Grab last 6 s Pulls [now − 6 s, now] out of the ring by passthrough export A normal captured shot with trigger.source: "manual". No penalty — a manual extraction is the same bytes as an automatic one
5 Two shots inside the 800 ms refractory a second onset inside the refractory Tray: Possible extra shot. / Two strikes landed close together. Tap to pull the second one out. One tap extracts the second window from the ring Without this row the session silently loses a shot, which corrupts M1
6 Nothing detected for 90 s while capturing timer Toast: No shots detected. / Check the framing — we need the ball and your whole body in shot. Opens the framing HUD's diagnostics No shot exists. This is a framing failure surfacing as silence, which is why it needs its own row
7 Disk / ring pressure, thermal critical free space < 1 GB at a segment rotation; thermalState == .critical [SPEC] Storage is full. / Free some space or end the session — we won't record over your shots.
Thermal: The phone is too hot to film cleanly. / Give it two minutes in the shade. Everything you've shot is saved.		 Session → paused; captured shots kept. At 80 % storage or thermal serious, a non-blocking banner offers End session first Stop recording rather than drop frames. Dropped or duplicated frames poison the time base, which is row 20's root cause
8 Second ball in scene preflight: > 1 persistent white circular candidate in the 2 s pre-roll. Backstop: more than one rest-ball candidate survives departure verification [MEASURED — a hanging spare ball was a persistent false-lock source for all three prototype trackers] Two balls in shot. / Clear the spare balls out of frame — we can lock onto the wrong one. Blocking at preflight in Ranked mode: the session stays armed with triggers disarmed until one ball is in the zone. Post-hoc: the contact-frame crop is shown with That's not my ball Normally blocked before a shot exists. If it slipped through: quality_flags += ["multiple_balls"], claim_point_speed=false. "That's not my ball" → refused + MULTIPLE_BALLS, and the clip joins the tracker regression set
9 Wrong orientation preflight: device is portrait [SPEC] Turn the phone sideways. / Landscape keeps you, the ball's whole arc, and the goal in one frame. Blocking rotation prompt; expo-screen-orientation locks landscape for the whole capture route No shot recorded while blocked
10 Low light ladder cannot reach ranks 1–3 without ISO > 2000, or contact-ROI contrast < 25/255 [SPEC]. Backstop: median detection confidence < 0.5 [PROPOSED] It's getting dark. / We can still film, but the numbers will be a range instead of an exact figure. Bright, even light gets you an exact one. Non-blocking. Persistent amber chip; the ladder drops to rank 6 (1080p60 at 1/240 s) and writes blur_suspected so the ruler bias is visible downstream rather than silent Shot proceeds. low_light flag; c_frame takes a WARN multiplier so the tier typically lands C. If median confidence < 0.5, claim_point_speed=false
11 Kicker not fully in frame preflight: body-outline guide unsatisfied [PROPOSED]. Authoritative at pose: S5 refuses a window where the kicker's feet leave the frame at contact [SPEC] We couldn't see all of you. / Step back so your whole body is in frame — we read the moment of contact from your kicking leg. The preflight guide, and on shot detail the contact-frame still with the bbox drawn so the crop is visible Pose lane refused → status='ready' with no posture block and no mesh. Do not render a partial mesh: a mesh fitted to a cropped body is the "default read as a measurement" bug wearing a 3D costume. The ball lane is unaffected

B. Registration and field profile (owner: the registration flow and the re-verify screen — the highest-risk UX in the product, so the flow failing gets rows, not only its outcomes)

# Failure Detected by Copy Recovery Disposition
12 AR tracking lost / coplanar anchor trackingState != .normal; no plane after 6 s or normal > 8° from gravity; all tapped points coplanar [SPEC] Tracking: Move the phone slowly and keep the ground in view.
Coplanar: Add the crossbar corners. / Four points on a flat rectangle can't tell distance from zoom.		 Capture disabled while tracking is lost. Coplanar is a blocking hint, not advice — four coplanar corners make focal and depth inseparable, which the detector already names goal_fronto_parallel [MEASURED]. Anchors are preserved across a tracking loss; never silently re-anchor No field profile is written. The user never reaches a session on a profile we do not trust
13 Calibration closure fails closure_width_err_pct at save. ≤ 2 % accept · 2–5 % accept degraded · > 5 % reject [PROPOSED — UNVALIDATED; task C-2 moves these to the measured 90th/99th percentiles over 20 registrations]. Crossbar uses 4 % / 8 % Accept: Measured 7.29 m against 7.32 m — good.
Degraded: Close enough to analyse, not close enough to rank. / Redo the walk in better light and this field can hold ranked records.
Reject: That walk drifted. / Do it again in better light, and don't cover the camera.		 Reject → re-register from step 2. Degraded → the profile saves with closure_grade='degraded' and the copy states the consequence up front, not after the first ranked shot fails degraded → c_cal = 0.6 → tier B/C → speed does not rank. A rejected walk writes no profile
14 Field profile stale older than 30 days [PROPOSED]; different device_model; goal dims edited; calibration_schema_version bumped; or > 3 consecutive camera_moved shots [PROPOSED] Let's re-check this field. / You last set up {field_name} on {date}. A 30-second re-check keeps your shots rankable. Re-verify in framing-only mode: goal dimensions and shooting distance are reused — the reuse promise, and what makes M6 reachable Shots on a stale profile get field_profile_stale and are not retroactively promoted after re-verification
15 LiDAR unavailable scene reconstruction unsupported [SPEC] First time only, dismissible forever: Your phone doesn't have a depth sensor — that's fine. / We'll use the goal's known size and your camera instead. Route to the goal-dimension preset picker No effect on eligibility. A non-LiDAR device reaches tier A like a Pro one — conditional on task C-2 (§5.3), which is the honest form of this row

C. Vision lanes (owner: the shot-map chips and the shot-detail screen; every code-level signal was verified against pipeline/ball.py)

# Failure Detected by Copy Recovery Disposition
16 Ball not found RuntimeError("only N real detections — refusing to fit") below 8 detections at conf ≥ 0.3; or no rest-ball candidate produced a departing track [MEASURED]. Soft tier 8 ≤ N < 20 emits only_N_detections_speed_is_band_only We lost the ball. / We found it for only part of the flight. Film from the side, 5–10 m back, with the ball clearly against the background.
KO: 공을 놓쳤어요. / 비행의 일부만 잡혔어요. 옆에서 5–10 m 뒤에서, 배경과 잘 구분되게 찍어 주세요.		 Shot detail overlays the tracked segment on the clip so the user sees where we lost it, plus the top quality tip. Offer Retake this shot < 8: refused + TOO_FEW_DETECTIONS, clip kept and replayable. 8–19: ready, claim_point_speed=false. Never retried automatically — retrying deterministic code on the same bytes gives the same answer
17 Ball leaves frame before apex track_truncated_at_frame_{t} or ball_below_5px_radius_track_ended where t precedes the fitted apex; track truncates after 6 consecutive misses rather than reporting noise [MEASURED] The ball left the frame too early. / Stand further back or turn the phone landscape — we need the ball from your boot up to the top of its arc. Landscape hint + the framing overlay with the arc corridor drawn (F10). Offer Retake claim_point_speed=false, claims.apex=false. The fit window is too short to condition the ruler: at 30 fps a 0.35 s window carries 8.7–28.9 % scale error depending on centroid noise, and a 0.6 s window drops the σ = 3 px case to 2.2 % [MEASURED — "a longer window beats a faster camera"; the source gives no σ = 10 px figure at 0.6 s]
18 Contact occluded 6 consecutive misses spanning contact_frame, or ball radius below the floor at contact [MEASURED]. Pose cross-check: contact_source: "disagreed" above 33 ms [SPEC] We couldn't see the moment of contact. / Your boot covered the ball. Move the camera around 30–60° to the side of the kick. The side-on framing overlay. Shot detail still renders the 3D mesh and the impact posture claim_point_speed=false. The pose lane survives and carries the product. Contact frames were the prototype's weakest detections at ±10–25 px, and contact anchors launch speed
19 Goal not visible, or fronto-parallel goal is None / goal["depth_m"] is None → metric_reference == false → claims.point_speed == false [MEASURED]. Adjacent: goal_fronto_parallel at asymmetry < 4 % and crossbar angle < 2.5° No goal in frame — this is an estimate. / With the goal in shot we can measure the distance directly and give you an exact speed. Without it, we show a range.
KO: 골대가 화면에 없어요 — 추정값이에요. / 골대가 함께 찍히면 거리를 직접 재서 정확한 속도를 낼 수 있어요. 없으면 범위로 보여 드려요.		 The band card carries Get an exact number → into registration. In Ranked mode, F2 blocks until all four corners sit inside the frame with ≥ 24 px margin claim_point_speed=false — Quick Mode's normal, correct state. Not a retry: the clip was analysed honestly, it just has no metric reference. no_metric_reference reason. goal_fronto_parallel → the goal contributes scale but not focal; tier capped at C [PROPOSED]
20 Frame drops / retimed clip three independent shipped refusals: duplicate_frame_ratio ≥ 0.5; VFR via pts_interval_iqr_ratio > 0.05; container-rate disagreement > 2 % — avg 30 vs r 239.02 is the literal AirDrop-baked-slo-mo signature [MEASURED] We can't trust this clip's timing. / Speed is calculated from the frame rate, and this file's frame rate doesn't add up. Record inside STRYKE instead of sharing a clip in — we'll get it exact. Deep-link to capture. Never offer "analyse anyway with a wider band" — an 8× rate error is an 8× speed error, and the band did not cover truth by 2× on a real retimed clip RETIMED_SUSPECTED for every speed surface including the band; the trajectory shape, video and mesh still render. Never eligible. This row cannot fire in v1's product path — import is P1 and v1 is in-app capture only — so it is exercised by the validation set's capture-hygiene fixtures and a be-vision unit test on a deliberately AirDropped 240 fps clip
21 Low-confidence fit — the band is the answer claims.point_speed == false with speed_refusal_reason; and/or basin_ambiguous with apex_refusal_reason; and/or railed_parameters non-empty — the parameter bound stopped the solve, not the data [MEASURED] Speed card renders the band with a tappable Why a range?. Sheet: We measured your shot as 29–77 km/h. / To give one exact number we need a known distance in frame. Right now the two ways we estimate depth disagree by more than we're willing to hide.
KO: 이번 슛은 29–77 km/h로 측정했어요. / 정확한 숫자 하나를 내려면 화면 안에 길이를 아는 기준물이 필요해요.		 Below the sheet copy, the literal speed_refusal_reason string, formatted. Show me how → opens registration. No retry offered — the answer is deterministic ready, claim_point_speed=false. apex_m and elevation_deg are not rendered. The map draws all surviving basins as ghost ribbons, never rank 1 alone. Arc shape, curl direction and the spin type chip still render
22 Pose lane failed, ball lane succeeded pose job terminal failed, ball succeeded [SPEC] On the mesh tile only: 3D replay unavailable. / Your numbers and the arc are all here. Tap to retry the body replay. Retry the pose lane only — a separate job row with its own idempotency key, so a retry costs one GPU minute and nothing else. The mesh card degrades to the 2D pose overlay status='ready'. Speed, band, placement, trajectory and eligibility are all unaffected. Distinct from row 11 (pose refused, correctly) and row 25 (whole pool down)

D. Transport and jobs

# Failure Detected by Copy Recovery Disposition
23 Upload interrupted a part PUT fails, or the app backgrounds mid-upload; be-api sees committed parts with no complete after 15 min Inline on the map row, never a modal: Waiting for signal — we'll finish uploading this shot automatically.
After 3 failed resumes in 24 h: Couldn't upload this shot. / The clip is still on your phone. Tap to try again.		 Automatic resume at the part boundary, not at zero, on reachability regain or a BGProcessingTask wake. Backoff 1/2/4/8/30 s then park. Manual Retry now uploading → upload_failed → pending_upload. The local clip is never deleted until the server acknowledges the object. An upload has no bearing on measurement quality, so nothing about tier or eligibility changes
24 Analysis timeout — one job reaper every 15 s: deadline_at passed, or heartbeat stale > 30 s. Deadlines ball 120 s / pose 600 s / render 300 s; max 3 attempts; backoff 30 s → 2 min → 8 min ±20 %; only infrastructure is retried Progress copy escalates against the ball lane, which is what the user is waiting on. At 45 s: Still working. / First shot of the day takes a bit longer while the analysis server wakes up. At the conditional threshold (180 s warm / 300 s cold): This one's taking too long. / We'll keep trying in the background and notify you when it's done. Automatic re-enqueue; APNs silent push on settle queued → dispatched → running → retry_wait → queued; after attempt 3, failed(retries_exhausted). failed is never shown as a bad shot — it is infrastructure, worded as such, and the clip stays replayable
25 Pose pool down (global) no pose-lane heartbeat for 90 s [PROPOSED], or pose queue depth > 20 with zero dispatched transitions in 120 s [PROPOSED] Banner: 3D replay is running late. / Your shots and numbers are landing normally — the body replay will fill in shortly.
KO: 3D 리플레이가 늦어지고 있어요. / 슛과 숫자는 정상적으로 들어오고 있어요. 몸 동작 리플레이는 곧 채워집니다.		 Capture and the ball lane are untouched. Push when the backlog drains. Ops note: scale-to-zero cold start is ~2 min BY DESIGN, so the banner must not fire before 90 s or it cries wolf on every first shot Shots reach partial — arcs appear, speeds land, placement lands — then ready with the mesh tile explaining. Saying "analysis is paused" here would tell the user the opposite of what they are watching happen
26 Ball lane down (global) the CPU pool is not draining queued ball jobs [PROPOSED]. This is the total-outage case, because the map needs only the ball lane Banner: Analysis is paused. / Keep shooting — every clip is saved and we'll analyse them all as soon as we're back. Usually a few minutes. Capture stays fully functional; jobs accumulate. Push when the backlog drains Every shot stays analysing. Nothing discarded, nothing downgraded. Do not fall back to FakeVisionPipeline in production — it returns a plausible speed_mps=24.3, confidence=0.91, and shipping that to a user is the single worst instance of this document's bug class. The prod guard is the mitigation

E. Serve — eligibility and disputes

# Failure Detected by Copy Recovery Disposition
27 "Why isn't my shot on the board?" the eligibility evaluator writes reasons, machine-readable and ordered by fixability Header: This shot isn't ranked. Then the first fixable reason only, rendered from its hint: register_field → Register this field once and shots here will rank. · get_goal_in_frame → Get the goal in shot. · film_from_the_side → Film from the side. · verify_email → Verify your email. Unfixable reasons are stated plainly, not dressed as advice: Rankings aren't live yet. The explainer reads reasons and eligible_metrics — never shot.status, never a generic help page. Because eligibility is per metric, the honest sentence is often "Your placement ranks; your speed doesn't yet", which status alone cannot express Nothing changes about the shot. One row per metric, no composite verdict
28 User disputes a number long-press any stat → Report this number + a required one-tap reason Thanks — this helps. / We'll compare this clip against our reference measurements. If we got it wrong, we'll tell you and fix the shot. Writes a shot_dispute with the claimed value typed and named as an opinion, not a measurement, and extends the clip's retention. A clip enters the truth set only after re-measurement The shot is unchanged pending review. If review confirms an error the shot takes invalidated_reason and the user is told: We re-checked your shot from {date} and our number was off. We've removed it from the rankings. Never silently edit a number the user has already seen

Two failures the catalogue deliberately does not include. "Analysis was wrong but confident" — there is no detector for this and pretending otherwise is the bug class itself; it is handled by row 28 plus the radar set. "Shot was bad" — a weak or mis-hit shot is a result, not a failure, and it gets numbers like any other shot.

9.4 The guards that make the honesty rules fail loudly

The recurring bug class, stated once with its evidence: workstream 24 produced "a default read as a measurement" four times in one afternoon (source: …/24-stryke-match-analytics/README.md, log 2026-08-19) — converged: true meaning "never checked"; rig_inconsistent emitted under a key name nobody guessed, so the first consumer read None; warn answering "is the number large" instead of "is something wrong"; and a defaulted chi² of 1.0 setting the floor beneath the aligned population, which made the operator's only calibration tool invert its own verdict. The adopted fix is the measured / null pattern, not more assertions.

Five rules and three layers of guard:

  1. No numeric field carries a non-null default. Optional with None, plus a reason string.
  2. No boolean named after a value — name it after the decision. warn failed because it answered "is the estimate large" when the operator's question was "is the output telling me something is wrong". Ours are claim_point_speed, is_verified, metric_reference, camera_moved, launch_in_zone, kicker_fully_in_frame — each the answer to a question a human actually asks.
  3. Units live in the field name, not in a unit: str. speed_band_kmh, apex_m, elevation_deg, distance_m. A free-text unit invites "kmh" vs "km/h" vs "kph".
  4. The validator closes both directions — a number without its claim and a claim with nothing behind it (§7.2).
  5. The CI guard operates on raw fixture JSON, not on objects the model built. A model_validator(mode="after") already refuses to construct a violating object, so a test that builds one and re-checks it cannot fail. The real lesson was cross-structure checking:
@pytest.mark.parametrize("path", sorted(FIXTURES.glob("*.json")))
def test_every_fixture_validates(path: Path) -> None:
    """Fixtures are hand-written or captured from the worker — never built through the model.
    A fixture that carries a number without its claim fails HERE, which is the point."""
    AnalysisResult.model_validate_json(path.read_text())

def test_db_projection_agrees_with_the_result(session, ball_fixture) -> None:
    """Compare two structures, not one against itself."""
    shot = apply_result(session, ball_fixture)
    assert (shot.ball_speed_kmh is None) == (ball_fixture.ball.claims.point_speed is False)
    assert (shot.ball_speed_low_kmh, shot.ball_speed_high_kmh) == ball_fixture.ball.speed_band_kmh
    assert shot.claim_point_speed == ball_fixture.ball.claims.point_speed

The database is the third layer: ck_shot_no_unclaimed_speed makes the violation unstorable.

  1. quality_flags → recovery copy is mapped in the app, not in the contract. be-vision emits QualityFlag and RefusalReason enum members and nothing else — there is no recovery: list[RecoveryHint] in the shared contract. If the worker emitted copy routing, a copy change would cross the independently-deployed contracts/ boundary and cost a CONTRACT_VERSION bump plus a worker deploy.
  2. The prod guard, which needs a setting the config does not have yet:
class Settings(BaseSettings):
    environment: str = "dev"          # dev | stage | prod
    vision_pipeline: str = "fake"     # unchanged default

# in the app lifespan
if settings.environment == "prod" and settings.vision_pipeline == "fake":
    raise RuntimeError("VISION_PIPELINE=fake refuses to serve prod")

This does not violate the scaffold's trap ("VISION_PIPELINE=fake is the default … do not make prod code paths assume a real worker"): the default stays fake, every laptop keeps working, and only environment=prod refuses it.

The durable lesson behind all of it: the value-shaped version was caught every time by RUNNING the thing, never by reading it. Which is why every stage in §10 has an exit criterion you run, not one you review.

10 Staged implementation plan

10.1 The team — seven lanes

Lane Who Notes
mobile — capture 1 engineer Swift: stryke-capture (AVFoundation, segmented ring, shot detection, FramingMonitor, sidecar, uploader) and stryke-calibrate (ARKit ritual). The hard half, device-only, no simulator
mobile — app 1 engineer RN/Expo: routes, local store, upload drain, <ShotScene>, shot detail, <MeasuredStat>
be-api 1 engineer FastAPI, alembic, Supabase, contracts, jobs + reaper, eligibility, rankings
be-vision 1 engineer ball.py + GVHMR in apps/be-vision, the worker loop, Modal ops, the P2 speed pass, the GLB bake
CV research 0.5 through Stage 1, 1 from Stage 2 Labelling + the diameter regressor, BlurBall, hypothesis enumeration. A second head, not the be-vision engineer wearing a hat
design 1 designer at 50 % Screens, bilingual copy, the recovery-sheet system, share assets
validation founder/lead at 50 %, 100 % during Stage 1's filming weeks Owns the radar set, the placement rig, the licensing decision, the FTO review, and every accuracy claim that leaves the building
content (JK) named owner, non-engineering Films JK's record library. It is P0 and it has a lead time no engineer can compress

Weeks are calendar weeks for this team working in parallel lanes. "3 weeks" means 3 calendar weeks, not 3 person-weeks.

Resolved

Resolved: the mobile engineer-week gap. 02 §11 prices the mobile build at 28.5 engineer-weeks across nine deliverables, and flagged that this does not fit ~16 calendar weeks for one engineer. The two-lane split above is the resolution (option 1 of the three that were named): capture (Swift, steps 2/5/6 ≈ 11 ew) and app (RN, steps 3/4/7/8/9 ≈ 16.5 ew) are cleanly separable, and "one file, one writer" is satisfiable with that split. The two options not taken, and why: cut P0 screens is available as a contingency but spends product scope before engineering has been tried; ship Stages 0–2 on expo-camera is the worst of the three — it builds a capture path that must be thrown away and accepts intrinsics.source: "diagonal_heuristic" (a ±15 % focal error that transfers 1:1 to speed, worth a 2.4× solved-speed span) throughout. stryke-capture v0 therefore lands in Stage 0, not Stage 5, which is what the mobile build order already recommended against the older plan.

10.2 The stages

Stage 0 — The demo, and the falsification spikes (3 weeks)

Goal: the BRIEF §10 sequence works end to end on one physical phone, in Quick Mode, against VISION_PIPELINE=fake and then against one real pre-baked analysis — and the decisions that would be expensive to reverse are settled with a measurement.

Exit criterion (a demo you can show). On a real iPhone, off a real pitch clip: shoot three times → three trajectories accumulate on the map → tap the second → the video replays with the arc drawn over it → the 3D mesh rotates → "Your hardest strike today — 41–46 km/h" with the Estimated pill on the number and +4 km/h vs your best at this field under it → one qualitative limiting-factor item → the session summary shows the delta against the previous shot. No hand-waving, no simulator, no localhost. Run it twice in front of someone who has never seen it.

Two beats from BRIEF §10 are deliberately not in this demo: "speed top 12 %" is substituted (Stage 0 runs against the fake pipeline, so the only way to render a percentile here is to hard-code the string, which is two of this plan's own risks committed in week 2), and the keeper simulation is cut — "do not build a fake keeper simulation to make a video; that number would immediately become the thing users quote."

Plus three spike gates, each answered with a number and an ADR:

Spike Gate
VisionCamera falsification Can we read kCMSampleBufferAttachmentKey_CameraIntrinsicMatrix from a frame processor at the rank-1 format with stabilization off, does its writer preserve PTS, and does it avoid worklets-core? If all three: reopen the stryke-capture decision
Intrinsics support across the ladder isCameraIntrinsicMatrixDeliverySupported == true reported per ladder rank on an iPhone 12 and a 15 Pro. False on a rank ⇒ that rank is deleted from the Ranked ladder. False on every rank ⇒ the whole argument for building this module collapses
The 3D renderer Build first: does the candidate build on RN 0.86 new-arch, and does react-native-worklets-core enter the lockfile? Then, on the survivors: ≥ 55 fps on a 51-frame vertex-animated GLB, ≤ 180 MB with 20 ribbons + 1 mesh, ≤ 400 ms to first frame, on an iPhone 12
Lane Work
mobile — capture The spikes, then stryke-capture v0: configure, preview, start/stop, segmented ring, sample-passthrough extraction, sidecar with real per-frame K + PTS indexed to clip frames, manual "Grab last 6 s". No detection. Gate: a 3.5 s rank-2 .mov with stabilization: off, that sidecar, the measured bitrate per ladder rank, and 8 minutes of continuous rank-2 capture on an iPhone 12 with 0 dropped frames, thermal trace attached
mobile — app Routes; the shot map with an accumulating trajectory renderer behind <ShotScene>; shot detail with video + mesh viewer. Render GLB, not PLY
be-api Sessions and shots; the multipart upload with upload_session/upload_part; the stage-progress surface
be-vision Extend FakeVisionPipeline to emit all four v2 shapes deterministically by shot_id hash — those are the states the app spends most of its time in. Plus the vertex-animated GLB exporter to the §4.4 contract, and one real ball_flight.json + GLB produced by hand as fixture #2, so the demo shows both the fake and one real result
CV research 0.5 head, supporting the rig build
design The three demo screens at production fidelity; the Estimated pill; the recovery-sheet component (empty here, filled in Stage 3)
validation Order the Pocket Radar in week 1 — it has a lead time and Stage 1 cannot start without it. Buy the second tripod, gaffer tape and tape measure. Open the licensing decision
content Scout and schedule the JK filming day

Depends on: nothing. Blocks: everything. Trap: the demo must run against the real contracts/ types, not FE-local mocks — a demo built on mocks silently diverges from the only surface the two backends share.

Stage 1 — Radar and placement ground truth (3 weeks, weeks 1–3, concurrent with Stage 0)

Goal: convert every accuracy statement from assertion to measurement. It depends on the radar arriving and on nothing else — the set runs against today's ball.py as a CLI over a clip file (with S4-1 landed). No Modal, no queue, no mobile app, no ported worker. Making this stage depend on the pipeline port would push the cheapest, highest-value, ranking-gating item from week 1 to roughly week 8, which is exactly the failure mode it exists to warn about.

Exit criterion: Protocol A Run 1 (§8.1, 156 kicks across 10 OFAT + 3 interaction cells), Protocol B on the same afternoon (§8.2), the repeatability study (§8.4), and the report committed in the fixed directory structure so runs are diffable. A3′, A5, A12, A13 measured and reported — none of them blocks Stage 2.

Lane Work
validation Owns this stage. Filming day, the CSV log, the goal-camera homography, the Bland–Altman / ICC / CV / coverage / placement analysis notebook, the published report
be-vision Task S4-1 first (widen the az bound; Run 1 must not be filmed before it). Then Tier-1 upgrades in parallel, each re-measured as it lands: read the QuickTime mebx timed-metadata track for real focal/shutter/readout; verify the shipped Reynolds-dependent Cd(v); a ground-crossing penalty in the loss; ray-discretised hypothesis enumeration replacing the 9-seed multi-start, to turn the basin coin-flip into a reportable posterior
be-api shot_dispute + validation_sample so the dataset lives next to the code, not in someone's Drive
mobile — app The ball-size one-tap (3/4/5), which kills a silent 7–14 % diameter-ruler bias
CV research Assists the filming day; starts labelling against the captured set

Depends on: radar delivery. Blocks: the rankings launch (hard), Run 2, and any marketing that states an accuracy number.

Stage 2 — Real pipeline behind the port (4 weeks)

Goal: ModalVisionPipeline replaces FakeVisionPipeline for real clips; both lanes return a real AnalysisResult.

Exit criterion, over 20 consecutive real clips with per-stage timings in job metadata:

Measurement Target
Worker-side mandatory path S1–S6, warm ≤ 40 s today, ≤ 25 s after the preprocess pass
S7 renders, on tap, one style ≤ 15 s
Ball lane, upload complete → partial p50 20 s / p90 35 s
Pose lane, warm p50 45 s / p90 75 s; cold p90 240 s
User-visible M2a (last frame → arc on the map) p50 ≤ 30 s
User-visible M2b (last frame → ready) p50 ≤ 40 s
Lane Work
mobile — app Local store + both FSMs + the session tray + thumbnails + the storage cap (gate: capture 10 shots in airplane mode, kill the app, reopen — all 10 present with thumbnails, and the media directory flagged excluded-from-backup). The upload pipeline: presigned parts, background URLSession, part-level resume, the drain, sidecar_uri (gate: kill the app mid-upload on cellular; it resumes at the part boundary. Measure the 20-shot drain on LTE and Wi-Fi against M2c)
be-api ModalVisionPipeline; the job table, the two-lane split, the reaper, SSE, worker callbacks with version dispatch; status derivation; the two confidence scores; the prod guard
be-vision Port ball.py + the GVHMR runner into apps/be-vision behind the worker loop; the CONTRACT_VERSION bump with a backward read of v1 for one release; warm model residency. The P2 speed pass: FP16 ViTPose/HMR2, YOLO stride-2/3 + box interpolation, shared decode across the session, larger batches — preprocess 39.3 s → 8–12 s. Re-run the Stage 1 set against the ported pipeline as a regression (a cheaper, different task from building the set)
CV research Map every quality.warnings string ball.py can emit to a QualityFlag member and thence to one recovery hint. A table, not research — and it is the input Stage 3 needs
design The full bilingual recovery-sheet copy deck (all 28 rows), EN drafted + KO reviewed
validation Sign off the copy deck. Nothing ships in shot detail that the validation lead has not read

Depends on: Stage 0. Blocks: Stages 3, 4.

Stage 3 — Honesty layer (3 weeks)

Goal: every failure in §9 is detected, surfaced with its exact copy in both languages, and dispositions the shot correctly. Every [PROPOSED] threshold is calibrated or explicitly carried forward with a named owner.

Exit criterion (a demo you can show, plus a green build). A scripted adversarial session on a real pitch that deliberately triggers at least 20 of the 28 rows in one sitting — knock the tripod, nudge it slightly, put a spare ball out, film portrait, film into the sun, film with no goal, step half out of frame, clap without shooting, shoot twice fast, stand still for 90 s, fill the disk, walk the calibration badly, backdate a profile, open the rankings explainer — and screen-record the result. Every one produces the right copy, the right disposition, and zero fabricated numbers. Five more rows come from fault injection on the same build (24, 25, 26, 7, 22); row 20 is a be-vision unit test on a deliberately AirDropped clip; row 28 is a manual QA pass.

Plus the detection gate: 30 real shots on a pitch — ≥ 28 confirmed, ≤ 2 false positives, 0 unrecoverable misses, and Instruments showing p99 ≤ 3 ms/frame on the capture queue with 0 dropped frames over 8 minutes. A dropped frame is a duplicate-frame refusal, so the accuracy gate is meaningless without the CPU gate. Plus make check green with both contract tests over the fixture corpus.

Lane Work
mobile — capture Shot detection: audio onset + luma confirm + the corrected fusion (with the speed-of-sound correction) + the three refusals; FramingMonitor at its real rates with the amber tier
mobile — app The recovery-sheet host; the Why a range? sheet; <MeasuredStat> as the only component that renders a number; preflight checks; "Grab last 6 s"; the session tray
be-api Tier + eligibility evaluation; reasons written on every settle; disputes; the two contract tests
be-vision Emit QualityFlag / RefusalReason members only — no copy, no hints, no RecoveryHint in the contract. Confirm the three timebase refusals fire on the AirDropped clip
design Empty and failure states for every route; the mode / claim / tier / eligibility visual system
validation Sign off every user-facing string, EN and KO

Depends on: Stage 2. Blocks: any build that leaves the team, and Stage 4.

Stage 4 — Verified Ranked Mode (5 weeks)

Goal: BRIEF §6 in full — first-time field registration, shooting zones, stability conditions, profile reuse. The highest-risk UX in the product, priced accordingly.

Exit criterion (a demo plus three measured gates). Register a field once; shoot 10 shots; knock the camera deliberately at shot 6; the app auto-pauses; re-verify framing in under 30 s without re-entering goal dimensions or distance; end with shots 1–5 ranked, 6–7 invalidated with camera_moved and their personal bests superseded, 8–10 ranked again. Then close the app, come back the next day, and reuse the profile with a framing-only re-check.

Gate Number
Calibration closure (= A10) 90th pct width_err_pct ≤ 2 % over 20 registrations across 3 venues and 2 device classes, reported separately for LiDAR and non-LiDAR. This is task C-2, and it retires the [PROPOSED] tag on row 13
Basin disambiguation basin_ambiguous == false on ≥ 90 % of Ranked clips, baselined under the --magnus lateral default, and no railed solves
Re-verify Knock the tripod: the app pauses, re-verify re-locks in under 20 s, field_profile_id unchanged. Handoff attitude delta measured over 20 placements, with the 0.3° cut moved to the measured 90th percentile
Lane Work
mobile — capture stryke-calibrate: the nine-step walk, the framing HUD (goal-quad reticle, side-on wedge, ball-size ring, stillness bar, shutter/ISO chip), FramingMonitor wired to re-verify. This delivers the ritual that removes 2 of the 7 fit parameters and is the direct fix for the basin degeneracy
mobile — app The registration screens nested under the flow chrome; zone variants; LiDAR and non-LiDAR paths; rows 12–14 recovery
be-api field_profile + field_calibration_check + calibration_hash; staleness; the cohort key
be-vision Non-coplanar goal PnP + PnPf: the current PnP uses 4 coplanar corners and goes degenerate fronto-parallel; the fix is elevated crossbar corners plus ground-plane points, then a focal line-search initialised from the device prior. This is what makes metric_reference == true reliable, which is what ranking depends on. Plus known-distance ÷ elapsed-frames as a fourth per_anchor entry — independent of camera height, tilt, focal and the diameter ruler. Fit directly in the goal frame with pose and focal fixed
CV research The learned ball-diameter regressor — the single biggest published lever, 24–28 % → ~10 % relative depth error. Lands in Stage 5
design The registration flow, tested on a real pitch, not at a desk. Completion rate per step is the primary metric
validation Re-run the Stage 1 analysis against Ranked-mode clips specifically — that cell is the one rankings launch on
content JK filming day happens no later than here, at ≥ 2 registered field profiles, because a JK record is only comparable under a matching profile

Depends on: Stage 3, and Stage 1's analysis rig (not its final verdict). Blocks: Stages 6, 7.

Stage 5 — The depth ruler + licensing execution (6 weeks)

Goal: reach the practical monocular ceiling (±5–8 %) and be commercially clean. Note what is not here, because an earlier plan double-booked it: in-app capture landed in Stage 0 and the ARKit ritual in Stage 4. This is a be-vision and CV-research stage with a light mobile lane, which is what makes 6 weeks real.

Exit criterion: Protocol A Run 2 against the in-app-capture + ritual path, with CV ≤ 5 % in the side-on + goal-visible + Ranked cell, and A1–A13 all pass on that cell. Plus: a signed licence or a merged, green migration, evidenced by a document, not an intention.

Lane Work
be-vision BlurBall (MIT — the one liftable licence in the metric-3D stack) for streak-midpoint detection and blur-as-velocity; per-frame PTS time base + a rolling-shutter row model t_eff = t_frame + (row/H)·T_readout; integrate the diameter regressor; the licence migration if that path won
CV research 100 % allocation. Finish the regressor and its training set; own the BlurBall integration
be-api Tier rules updated for the in-app-capture anchor; a versioned migration for shots captured under the old ritual (verified_eligible is stored, so re-deriving it after a threshold change is a migration with a version stamp, not a query change)
mobile — app Consume the new anchors; surface nothing new — this stage should be invisible except that the bands get narrower
design The capture ritual's polish — it has to feel like 2 seconds, not a chore. Precedent: Rapsodo requires aim-and-level on every shot and consumers tolerate it
validation Runs Run 2; publishes it against Run 1 in the diffable format

Depends on: Stage 4. Blocks: any accuracy claim tighter than a band.

Stage 6 — Rankings launch (2 weeks) — hard-gated on Stage 1 / Run 2

Exit criterion. The acceptance gate passed for the launch cell, and the cohort satisfies all three publish conditions before any percentile renders. Until then the API returns percentile: null with rankings_not_launched and the product shows the ladder BRIEF §7 mandates.

Lane Work
be-api Cohort keying with unit tests on band boundaries; the nightly recompute with the direction-aware per-user cap; percentile as a binary search over 99 stored breaks — never a stored per-shot percentile; is_verified as a stored decision; backfill for shots recorded before the gate opened
mobile — app The rankings surface; the "why isn't my shot here" explainer, which reads reasons and eligible_metrics — not status, not a generic help page
design Leaderboard, per-item percentile rows, and the empty state before the cohort fills — which is the state most users see for months, so it is the design that matters most here
validation Publishes the accuracy report the rankings are justified by, and confirms the FTO review is closed. If we cannot publish it, we do not launch them

Stage 7 — Keeper sim + JK layer (3 weeks)

Exit criterion. Pick any ranked shot → Goalkeeper Challenge → pick a generic tier → the keeper's dive is driven by that shot's points_3d, replayable from three angles; and the result screen carries a 15–30 s JK tip selected from a multi-shot pattern, not from the single shot.

Hard prerequisite: the coaching selector may only use posture metrics that survived the repeatability study. Not flagged with a caveat: dropped.

Lane Work
mobile — app Keeper sim on the same <ShotScene>, second mesh entry in hologram; JK trajectory overlay; multi-angle replay. Disabled with an explanatory chip when basin_ambiguous is true — a simulation on an arc ambiguous across four basins is theatre
be-api The multi-shot coaching selector exactly as §4.6 specifies, in insights/service.py — the bounded context is insights and module naming is machine-enforced by import-linter. There is no coaching/ module
design Generic tiers only — Elite Keeper and Pro Keeper at P1 (club / youth are Later). Real names, likeness and motion data need rights we do not have
validation Save-probability copy review. Never stated as fact. The approved form is "Under the Elite Keeper reaction model, this shot beats the keeper about 64 % of the time."
Resolved

Resolved: keeper tiers. Three sets existed — elite|pro|club|youth (the column), "Amateur / Pro / Elite" (05 Stage 7 and 06), and 01's older "Amateur Keeper". The column's four values win, and P1 ships elite + pro only. "Amateur Keeper" is retired everywhere.

10.3 Dependency graph and the calendar arithmetic

week 1 ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────►
  Stage 0  (demo + spikes, 3w) ──┬──► Stage 2 (real pipeline, 4w) ──► Stage 3 (honesty, 3w)
  Stage 1  (radar, 3w) ══════════╪══════════════════════════════════════════╗    │
    ▲ needs only the radar        │                                         ║    ▼
    └─ ordered week 1             │                                    HARD GATE │
                                  │                                         ║  Stage 4 (verified, 5w)
  LICENCE DECISION (week 1) ──► signed or migrated ──► HARD GATE at         ║    ├──► Stage 6 (rankings, 2w) ◄══╝
                                  Stage 3 exit on any build leaving the team ║    └──► Stage 7 (keeper+JK, 3w)
                                                                            ╚═══► Stage 5 (depth ruler, 6w) ──► (Run 2)

Stage 0 (3) → Stage 2 (4) → Stage 3 (3) → Stage 4 (5) → Stage 6 (2)   =  17 weeks
+ 15% slack (2.5 w, rounded up)                                       =  ~20 weeks

Stage 1 (3 w) runs weeks 1–3 in the validation lane and costs 0 critical-path weeks — provided the radar is ordered in week 1. If it is not, it becomes the critical path, and that is the single cheapest scheduling mistake available to us. Stage 7 runs in parallel with Stages 5–6 in the mobile-app lane. If rankings are made to wait for Stage 5's tighter bands:

Stage 0 (3) → Stage 2 (4) → Stage 3 (3) → Stage 4 (5) → Stage 5 (6) → Stage 6 (2)  =  23 weeks
+ 15% slack                                                                        =  ~27 weeks

The 15 % is stated, not hidden, and it is the only slack in these numbers.

10.4 The licensing decision — on the critical path, at week 1

This is not a Stage 5 cleanup item. It gates every build that leaves the team, because the 3D body mesh is in the first thing anyone sees.

When What Owner
Stage 0, week 1 Answer: is STRYKE commercial? (It is — it has rankings and a JK layer.) Write it down validation
Stage 0, week 2 Request a Meshcapade quote and timebox a 3-day SAM-3D-Body spike, so the choice is made against a measured migration cost rather than a guess. In the same week, land the PoseBackend port — a joint-name-keyed boundary, so metrics.py reads joints["r_ankle"] and never learns a rig's integer index validation + be-vision
Stage 0, week 2 Get the GVHMR commercial-use answer in writing. Meshcapade licenses the model; it does not license GVHMR's code. A gate with no owner is not a gate tbg
Stage 1, week 3 Commission the FTO review of USPTO 10796437. It must close before Stage 6, whose exit is a public rankings launch with a marketed accuracy report validation
Stage 3 exit HARD GATE. No build goes to anyone outside the team — no external TestFlight, no investor demo on a device we do not control, no App Store submission — until either the Meshcapade licence is signed and row B is answered, or the migration is merged and green. Carve-out: an internal demo on a team-controlled device, operated by a team member, is permitted pre-gate. That is exactly what Stage 0's exit criterion is. Handing the device over, or installing the build on theirs, is not validation
Stage 5 If migrating, the migration lands here alongside the depth-ruler work, since both touch be-vision's model stack be-vision
Stage 6 FTO review closed and filed, or no marketed speed number validation
Resolved

Resolved: where the hard gate sits. 01 §7.1a places it at "Stage 2 exit"; 05 §4.4 places it at "Stage 3 exit". Stage 3 exit wins — 05 owns the plan and the stages were renumbered, and Stage 3 is the last stage before anything is demoed outside the team. 01's reference is to the pre-renumber ordering.

Also on the ship-blocking path and easy to forget: Ultralytics/AGPL detector weights are fine for prototyping but not for shipping — and scope B (the 2D-pose fallback) runs a detector too, which is why D1 must clear the 2D stack and not only GVHMR/SMPL.

10.5 Definition of done

A build is shippable to a stranger when all of these are true, and each is checkable in one command or one demo:

  1. make check green, including both contract tests — the raw-fixture validation and the DB-projection pair check — over the shot fixture corpus.
  2. The adversarial-session screen-recording is current for this build, with zero fabricated numbers across ≥ 20 triggered rows plus the five fault-injected ones.
  3. No user-facing surface renders a point value whose claim is false. A band is rendered only from speed_band_kmh, never as a midpoint, always with its refusal reason one tap away.
  4. No shot reaches is_verified = true without an active, non-stale field profile, claim_point_speed == true, speed_confidence >= 0.75, tier IN ('A','B'), band_width_pct <= 20, distance_source = 'fitted', launch_in_zone, foot_source = 'pose', and capture_source = 'in_app'.
  5. Every [PROPOSED] threshold has either been calibrated and re-tagged, or carries a named owner and a dated ticket. A [PROPOSED] number with neither does not ship.
  6. The licensing gate is satisfied — a signed licence or a merged migration, evidenced by a document — and the app renders GLB with no PLY loader anywhere in apps/fe-mobile.
  7. The FTO review is closed and filed, or the build markets no absolute speed number anywhere in the app, the store listing, or the site.
  8. Every accuracy claim in the app, the store listing and the marketing site traces to the validation report. If it does not appear there, it does not appear.
  9. Every user-facing string exists in EN and KO, and the KO has been read by the named reviewer.

10.6 Cross-document edits this merge requires

The resolutions above are only real if they land in code and in the section files. Each is a change to a named artefact, not a note:

# Where Edit
E1 contracts/, 04 §3.5, 03 §7 Split analysis_confidence into speed_confidence + pose_confidence; tier bands to A ≥ 0.75 / B 0.40 / C 0.20 / D < 0.20; confidence_threshold default 0.75; core/insights.py reads pose_confidence

11 Open decisions

These need a human answer. Each names what it blocks and when it must land; the engineering plan routes around none of them.

# Decision Owner Blocks Clock
D1 Body-mesh licensing. Buy a Meshcapade licence (clears the SMPL/SMPL-X model) and get a written GVHMR commercial-use answer (Meshcapade does not clear GVHMR's code) — or migrate to SAM 3D Body (commercial SAM licence, MHR rig, no OpenSim bridge yet) or GEM-X (Apache-2.0 code + NVIDIA Open Model weights — the weights licence is the half that decides it). And, separately: is the scope-B 2D-pose stack itself licence-clean? Ultralytics/AGPL detector weights sit on the ship-blocking path, so clearing GVHMR/SMPL alone does not clear scope B. Sub-question: does a decimated, rig-free, vertex-animated mesh derived from an SMPL-X fit require a Meshcapade licence to distribute? tbg + validation Which of the two P0 scopes we build (§3.2), and every build that leaves the team Commercial-or-not answered week 1; quote + 3-day SAM spike + GVHMR letter week 2; hard gate at Stage 3 exit
D2 Keeper rights. P1 ships generic tiers only (Elite Keeper, Pro Keeper). Do we ever pursue a named keeper's name + likeness + motion rights, or is the generic tier the permanent product? Until rights_cleared is true the API cannot serve a name, so this is a business decision, not an engineering one tbg Whether KeeperModel.rights_cleared is ever set; the keeper art direction Before the Stage 7 content freeze
D3 Supabase vs our own Postgres. The design assumes Supabase (Postgres + Storage + Auth), per the BRIEF's own technology table. That choice is load-bearing in three places: Auth (Apple + email OTP with JWKS validation), Storage (private buckets, per-prefix lifecycle, signed URLs), and RLS as defence in depth. The case to stay: auth and storage come free, and the app never talks to Postgres anyway (PostgREST is disabled and be-api holds the only credentials), so the lock-in surface is thin. The case to move: the storage budget is ≈ 7.9 GB rolling + 1.5 GB/year per active user at 20 shots/week with a 12 GB soft cap, which is where object-storage pricing and egress start to matter; and RLS is only worth having because of the SET LOCAL dependency we wrote ourselves, which works identically on any Postgres. Decide once, now, because the migration cost rises with every table. If we move, the shape is: our own Postgres + S3 + a hosted auth provider, and §4.2's request_session and §4.5's key scheme are unchanged tbg Nothing today — the design is portable — but the answer changes the Stage 2 infra work and the cost model Before Stage 2 starts
D4 Which radar condition cells do we validate first? The full matrix is not runnable (768 cells / 9,216 kicks), so §8.1 names an OFAT design with a fixed baseline. The genuinely open part is which baseline and which three interaction cells — the current answer is side-on / 1080p240 / tripod / goal-in-frame / Quick / bright / LiDAR, with down-axis × goal-absent, dusk × handheld, and retimed × baseline validation The rankings gate, percentiles, config.point_speed_released, M7 Week 1, with the radar order
D5 Hero style for v1 — pearl mesh, hologram, or user-selectable? Render one per job, never two. §4.4 answers it by use (mesh is the job default; hologram for the impact freeze, the keeper and the share render); this is the confirmation, and if it changes, only that table changes product + tbg Render latency and GPU cost Before Stage 2
D6 Is JK's record library filmed before launch, and at which comparison cells? Four JK screens are P0 and are empty without it, and a JK record is only comparable under a matching field profile — so it must be filmed at ≥ 2 registered profiles, not just "somewhere" content owner JKRecordsScreen, JKChallengeScreen, and the middle-to-top rungs of the benchmark ladder Filming day no later than Stage 4
D7 Who curates the stryke_verified benchmark rung, from what measurements, and what provenance string ships? It is source='curated' and never computed from user data, so at launch it is a hand-entered number sitting between two real ones on the honesty screen product + validation The middle rung of the P0 ladder. If unanswered, the ladder ships with two rungs (Your best · JK's record), not with an unsourced number Before the Stage 6 design freeze
D8 Quick Mode clip import at P1: allowed at all, or in-app capture only forever? Import re-opens the retime/8×-speed-error class and is the only path on which server-side segmentation and the 3-shots-per-clip cap exist product The shape of the P1 growth loop Before Stage 5
D9 Does Quick Mode get its own session-relative leaderboard, separate from Ranked? A Quick shot is tier B or C and verified_eligible == false, so it can never enter a Ranked cohort whatever the surface decides — but a Quick-only board is buildable today and needs no radar product A rankings surface, not a measurement Before Stage 6
D10 FTO review of USPTO 10796437. It "sits uncomfortably close to our claim set" and must complete before any marketed speed number tbg + outside counsel Stage 6's exit, which is a public rankings launch with a published accuracy report Commissioned Stage 1 week 3, closed before Stage 6
D11 Does stryke-pipeline/pipeline/ become apps/be-vision/, or stay a separate repo? §4.4's plan to promote the render constants into contracts/ assumes the former; if it stays separate, render.py consumes stryke-contracts as a published dependency and the generator runs in the monorepo only. Cheap either way — a CONTRACT_VERSION bump plus a make contracts generator — but nothing currently says which tbg + be-vision The render-spec generator's wiring; nothing else Before Stage 2

Three more that are engineering-owned but must not be forgotten, because each is currently an assumption wearing a number: task C-2 (tap σ and the closure distribution over 20 registrations, which is the input to the non-LiDAR fallback and to A10); task V-1 (refit the confidence weights against the Run 2 radar set, replacing the initial engineering values); and task V-2 (measure p₀ from population data — it may be raised from 0.30, never lowered below the 0.1336 implied by |z| ≥ 1.5). Until V-1 and V-2 land, every coaching claim rests on an assumed null and the weights are labelled as estimates in the report and never shown to users. w_m, the outcome weight, is unmeasured and seeded from the JK reference model — outcome_weight_source carries that provenance to the copy layer, and the copy layer has to actually say it.

One design question that is intentionally left to the volume data: whether a 2 m × goal × shot-type × foot × age cell ever reaches 30 distinct users. The parent-cohort ladder is the mitigation; re-cut the bins from the real distribution once 1,000 verified shots exist, and bump the v1 cohort prefix when you do — in core/cohort.py, so the coaching cell moves with it.

External literature and legal references

Reference What it establishes
Van Zandycke & De Vleeschouwer, CVPRW 2022, Table 2 Hough-circle diameter MAE 4.6–4.9 px → 24–28 % relative depth error — the baseline the diameter ruler must beat
Öztürk et al., JHSE 2023, n = 670 Athla Velocity ICC 0.970 with a +6.8 % systematic bias — on tennis serves, phone 5 m directly behind the server, with a metric corridor we do not have
Rico-González et al., J Sports Sci 2021, PMID 33377436 Pocket Radar validated against the Stalker ATS II for soccer kicks: ICC ≥ 0.98, bias ≤ 3.19 km/h, SDC ≤ 7.70 km/h — which is why A1's floor is 4 km/h
Sensors 2022, PMC9505007, n = 153 The Stalker's own repeatability on soccer kicks (CV 4.0–5.4 %, ICC 0.945–0.958) and the behind-vs-in-front collapse (CV 7.4–15 %, ICC 0.134–0.693, error 17.0–37.5 km/h) — the mounting rule
TT3D Corner MAE 2.39 ± 1.47 px; the focal/depth correlation under fronto-parallel geometry; side 12.4 cm / oblique 17.1 cm / back 29.8 cm under identical noise
GraviCap Ball-only self-calibration at 445–483 mm trajectory error — 5–10× worse than scene-anchored methods
WASB-SBDT, TrackNetV3, BlurBall (MIT) The only liftable licences in the ball stack; the v2 detector candidates
SMPL / SMPL-X (MPI, research-only) · Meshcapade The body model and its only purchasable commercial path
GVHMR (custom non-commercial, copyleft-on-modification) The pose backend, and the encumbrance Meshcapade does not clear
SAM 3D Body (Meta) · GEM-X (Apache-2.0 code + NVIDIA Open Model weights) The two migration candidates
USPTO 10796437 Sits close to our claim set; an FTO review belongs before any marketed speed number
